# Hezbollah–Israel conflict (2023–present)

This article is about the escalation that began in 2023 and led to the [Israeli invasion of Lebanon in 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israeli_invasion_of_Lebanon). For the general conflict, see [Hezbollah–Israel conflict](62111540.md).

An ongoing conflict between the Lebanese militant group [Hezbollah](13919.md) and [Israel](9282173.md) began on 8 October 2023, when [Hezbollah launched rockets and artillery at Israeli positions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_projectile_attacks_from_Lebanon_on_Israel_and_the_Golan_Heights) following [Hamas](13913.md)'s [October 7 attacks](74998317.md) on Israel. The conflict escalated into a prolonged exchange of bombardments, leading to extensive displacement in Israel and [Lebanon](17771.md). The conflict is part of the broader [Middle Eastern crisis](75895273.md) that began with Hamas's attack, with the short [Israeli invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israeli_invasion_of_Lebanon) of Lebanon in 2024 marking the largest escalation of the [Hezbollah–Israel conflict](62111540.md) since the [2006 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Lebanon_War). In Arabic, the conflict (2023–2024) is referred to as the "**Support War**" or the "**Gaza Support War**."[^cite_note-85]

On 8 October 2023, Hezbollah started firing guided [rockets](26301.md) and [artillery shells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artillery_shells) at Israeli positions in the [Shebaa Farms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shebaa_Farms), which it said was in solidarity with Palestinians following the 7 October Hamas-led attack on Israel and beginning of [Israeli bombing of the Gaza Strip](76139149.md).[^cite_note-Goldenberg-2023-86][^cite_note-Reuters-20231008-87] Israel retaliated by launching [drone strikes](38212231.md) and artillery shells at Hezbollah positions. Israel also carried out airstrikes throughout Lebanon and in Syria. In northern Israel, the ongoing conflict has forced approximately 96,000 individuals to leave their homes,[^cite_note-88][^cite_note-reuters20240111-83] while in Lebanon, over 1.4 million individuals had been displaced by late October.[^cite_note-89][^cite_note-90] Hezbollah stated it would not stop attacks against Israel until it stops its [military operations in Gaza](74998519.md);[^cite_note-aljazeera20240911-91] Israel said its attacks would continue until its citizens could return safely to the north.

In September 2024, Israel intensified its operations with two waves of [electronic device attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Lebanon_electronic_device_attacks) targeting Hezbollah's communication systems, and later assassinated the group's leading figures, including Secretary-General [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md),[^cite_note-apnews20240928-92] and his successor, [Hashem Safieddine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hashem_Safieddine). On 1 October, the Israeli military began an [invasion of southern Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israeli_invasion_of_Lebanon),[^cite_note-Third-War-93] although it had been conducting limited ground operations for some time. Israeli operations led to the significant dismantling of Hezbollah's military infrastructure in southern Lebanon and the destruction of a large portion of its missile stockpile.[^cite_note-Economist2024-94]

A 60-day [ceasefire agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israel%E2%80%93Lebanon_ceasefire_agreement) was brokered and took effect on 27 November 2024.[^cite_note-upi20241127-95] The ceasefire required Hezbollah to move its fighters north of the [Litani River](1920925.md), approximately 30 kilometres (19 miles) from the Israeli border, while Israel began withdrawing its forces from southern Lebanon. The [Lebanese Army](17779.md) was tasked with deploying around 5,000 soldiers to monitor the situation and maintain peace in the region. The ceasefire is being monitored by a panel of five countries, led by the [United States](3434750.md), though Israel retains the right to strike at immediate threats in Lebanon during this period.[^cite_note-Economist2024-94] The ceasefire was extended to 18 February 2025, at which time the [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md) (IDF) withdrew from most of southern Lebanon. [Airstrikes resumed on 2 March 2026](82549840.md) between Hezbollah and Israel following the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md) and the [killing of Ali Khamenei](82537558.md). On 16 April, President Trump announced that Israel and Lebanon agreed to a 10-day [truce](82970933.md).[^cite_note-aljazeera20260416-96] On 23 April, Trump announced that Israel and Lebanon agreed to a three-week extension of the ceasefire.[^cite_note-97] On 15 May, the truce was extended by Israel and Lebanon for another 45 days.[^cite_note-reuters20260515-98] On 1 June, Israel and Hezbollah agreed to a ceasefire, with Israel committing not to target Beirut's southern suburbs and Hezbollah vowing not to attack Israel, under a US proposal aiming to extend the ceasefire to all of Lebanon.[^cite_note-thehindu20260601-99] On 3 June, Israel and Lebanon reached an agreement to renew the ceasefire, facilitated by the US, and to create "pilot zones".[^cite_note-firstpost20260604-100] On 4 June, Hezbollah rejected the previously announced truce deal, demanding instead a comprehensive truce and a full withdrawal of Israel from Lebanon.[^cite_note-aawsat20260604-101] On 19 June, President Trump announced that Israel and Hezbollah have reached a truce, facilitated by the US, Qatar, and Iran.[^cite_note-102][^cite_note-103] On 26 June, US Secretary of State [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) announced a framework deal between Israel and Lebanon for "lasting peace and security" via US mediation. The agreement includes a cease-fire, requiring Hezbollah to end hostilities and withdraw from southern Lebanon, but it was subsequently rejected by Hezbollah.[^cite_note-104][^cite_note-105]

## Background

See also: [Palestinian insurgency in South Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_insurgency_in_South_Lebanon), [Israeli–Lebanese conflict](6020082.md), and [Hezbollah–Israel conflict](62111540.md)

### Founding of Hezbollah

See also: [1982 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_Lebanon_War) and [South Lebanon conflict (1985–2000)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Lebanon_conflict_\(1985%E2%80%932000\))

Hezbollah is a Lebanese [Shiite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shiite) political party and paramilitary group, formed in 1982 by Muslim clerics with Iranian funding to fight the [Israeli invasion of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_Lebanon_War).[^cite_note-106] After the war, Israel continued to hold [borderland buffer zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_occupation_of_Southern_Lebanon_\(1982%E2%80%932000\)) in southern Lebanon with the aid of proxy militants in the [South Lebanon Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Lebanon_Army) (SLA).[^cite_note-Encarta:_Arab–Israeli_Conflict-107] In 2000, Israel withdrew from South Lebanon to the UN-designated and internationally recognized [Blue Line](987817.md) border.[^cite_note-108] Hezbollah quickly took control of the area. It has justified its continued existence, occupation, and attacks on Israel by citing both allegations of [Lebanese prisoners in Israel](6403729.md) and continued Israeli control of the [Shebaa farms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shebaa_Farms) region, occupied by Israel from Syria [in 1967](29328.md) but considered by Hezbollah to be part of Lebanon.[^cite_note-Institute_for_Palestinian_Studies-109][^cite_note-Boston-110] [Elimination of the state of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calls_for_the_destruction_of_Israel) has been a primary goal for Hezbollah from its inception.[^cite_note-nybooks-111][^cite_note-112][^cite_note-113] Hezbollah opposes the government and policies of the State of Israel.[^cite_note-114][^cite_note-115]

### Palestinian factions in Lebanon

See also: [Palestinian insurgency in South Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_insurgency_in_South_Lebanon)

Since the [1948 Palestinian expulsion and flight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Palestinian_expulsion_and_flight), [Palestinian refugees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinians_in_Lebanon) have had a presence in [Southern Lebanon](4205968.md) as numerous refugee camps were established, which brought many Palestinian factions into south Lebanon, with it being often used as a center to [launch rockets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_projectile_attacks_from_Lebanon_on_Israel_and_the_Golan_Heights) into northern Israel. The [Palestine Liberation Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Liberation_Organization) was based in Lebanon after being [expelled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_September) from [Jordan](7515964.md) by [King Hussein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Hussein) in July 1971.[^cite_note-116] After they were involved in an [insurgency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_insurgency_in_South_Lebanon) in Southern Lebanon, until they were expelled to [Tunis](57659.md) after the [1982 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_Lebanon_War).[^cite_note-117]

### UN Security Council Resolution 1701

See also: [United Nations Security Council Resolution 1701](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_1701), [Blue Line (withdrawal line)](987817.md), and [Ghajar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghajar)

A ceasefire was reached between Israel and Hezbollah at the end of the [2006 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Lebanon_War), based on the terms of [UN Security Council Resolution 1701](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Security_Council_Resolution_1701) which called for a demilitarized zone between the southern border of Lebanon and the [Litani river](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Litani_river). The [UN Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Security_Council) resolution mandated only the Lebanese army and [United Nations Interim Force in Lebanon](787766.md) (UNIFIL) could be armed in southern Lebanon. It also stated that neither side should cross the [Blue Line](987817.md), which marks the border between Lebanon and the [Golan Heights](7515865.md), and divides the village of [Ghajar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghajar) between the two.[^cite_note-118][^cite_note-119] Despite this, both Israel and Hezbollah have outstanding obligations under UNSCR 1701.[^cite_note-120][^cite_note-jpost20240826-121][^cite_note-securitycouncilreport20230713-122]

Since 2006, Hezbollah fortified southern Lebanon,[^cite_note-123] obstructed UNIFIL's access, built tunnels into Israel, and crossed the Blue Line.[^cite_note-124][^cite_note-125] Israel has also accused Hezbollah of using a local environmental organization as a front for its military activities near the Blue Line.[^cite_note-126][^cite_note-127] After retreating behind the Blue Line in 2000,[^cite_note-csis20240321-128][^cite_note-129] Israel reoccupied the whole of [Ghajar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghajar) in 2006, including the Lebanese part of the village.[^cite_note-130][^cite_note-131] Israel continues to occupy Ghajar and an adjacent area,[^cite_note-securitycouncilreport20230713-122][^cite_note-csis20240321-128] and has repeatedly breached Lebanese airspace, waters, and borders.[^cite_note-132][^cite_note-133][^cite_note-134] UNIFIL reports that Israel entered Lebanese airspace on more than 22,000 occasions between 2007 and 2021.[^cite_note-135][^cite_note-136]

### April and July 2023 skirmishes

Main article: [April 2023 Palestinian rocket attacks on Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_2023_Palestinian_rocket_attacks_on_Israel)

On 6 April 2023, in response to the [2023 Al-Aqsa clashes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Al-Aqsa_clashes), dozens of rockets were fired from Lebanon into Israel, injuring three Israeli civilians.[^cite_note-Al-Jazeera-2023-137] The [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md) said that it intercepted 25 rockets fired from Lebanon,[^cite_note-Al-Jazeera-2023-137] which it said were fired by Palestinian factions [Hamas](13913.md) and [PIJ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Jihad_Movement_in_Palestine) with [Hezbollah](13919.md)'s approval.[^cite_note-CNN3-138] The attacks were the largest escalation between the two countries since the [2006 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Lebanon_War).[^cite_note-CNN3-138] The [United Nations Interim Force in Lebanon](787766.md) (UNIFIL) described the situation as "extremely serious" and urged restraint.[^cite_note-CNN3-138] On 15 July, the IDF fired warning shots and used riot dispersal munitions on 18 people, including journalists and parliamentarians that crossed the border from Lebanon and walked 80 meters into Israeli-occupied territory.[^cite_note-139]

### Gaza war

See also: [Gaza war](74998519.md)

On 8 October 2023, a day after [Hamas](13913.md) launched its [7 October 2023 attacks](74998317.md) on Israel and [Israel began its bombing of Gaza](76139149.md), [Hezbollah](13919.md) joined the conflict in "solidarity with the Palestinians",[^cite_note-aj9oct2023-140][^cite_note-141] initially firing on Israeli military outposts in [Shebaa Farms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shebaa_Farms) and the [Golan Heights](7515865.md)—both territories [under Israeli occupation](2781576.md).[^cite_note-aj9oct2023-140] Since then, Hezbollah and Israel have been involved in cross-border military exchanges that have displaced entire communities in Israel and Lebanon, with significant damage to buildings and land along the border. From 7 October 2023 to 20 September 2024, there were 10,200 cross border attacks, of which Israel launched 8,300.[^cite_note-142] Over 96,000 people in Israel,[^cite_note-reuters20240416-143] and over 111,000 in Lebanon, have been displaced during this period.[^cite_note-leb-displace-144] Israel and Hezbollah have maintained their attacks at a level that causes harm without escalating into a full-scale war.[^cite_note-145]

Hezbollah has stated it will continue attacking Israel until Israel halts [its operations in Gaza](74998519.md),[^cite_note-aljazeera20240911-91] where over 40,000 Palestinians had been killed.[^cite_note-146][^cite_note-147] Israel demanded that Hezbollah implement [UNSC 1701](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_1701) and withdraw its forces north of the [Litani River](1920925.md).[^cite_note-jpost20240826-121][^cite_note-148] Diplomatic efforts, led by U.S. envoy [Amos Hochstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amos_Hochstein) and France, have so far been unsuccessful in resolving the conflict.[^cite_note-149][^cite_note-150] In November 2023, Israeli defense minister [Yoav Gallant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Gallant) warned that [Beirut](37428.md) could meet the same fate as Gaza.[^cite_note-151] He made the same warning in January 2024.[^cite_note-152] In June 2024, Gallant visited the [United States](3434750.md), seeking support for an escalation of the war with Hezbollah and a possible ground invasion in [Lebanon](17771.md).[^cite_note-153]

## Events

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of Hezbollah–Israel conflict (2023–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Hezbollah%E2%80%93Israel_conflict_\(2023%E2%80%93present\)).

### Outbreak of fighting (October 2023 – March 2024)

IDF footage of strike on Hezbollah infrastructure in the [Shebaa Farms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shebaa_Farms)

In the morning of 8 October, [Hezbollah](13919.md) fired rockets and shells at the [Shebaa Farms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shebaa_Farms) region in support of [Hamas](13913.md)'s attack onto Israel; in response, the [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md) (IDF) fired artillery shells and a drone into southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-154][^cite_note-155][^cite_note-Asharq-AlAwsat-156] Two Lebanese children were reportedly injured by broken glass.[^cite_note-Goldenberg-2023-86]

The next day, Israel exchanged a series of airstrikes on southern Lebanon near the towns of [Marwahin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marwahin), [Ayta ash Shab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayta_ash_Shab)[^cite_note-157] and Dhayra in the [Bint Jbeil district](12027489.md).[^cite_note-Reuters-20231009-158] This was after numerous Palestinian militants infiltrated the Israeli border,[^cite_note-159] which Hezbollah denied involvement with. The [Palestinian Islamic Jihad](13093253.md) militia claimed responsibility for the armed infiltration. The IDF killed at least two perpetrators (likely Palestinians),[^cite_note-Reuters-20231009-158] while a third returned to Lebanon.[^cite_note-160] A Hezbollah media source announced three of Hezbollah's members died in the IDF retaliation.[^cite_note-161] Hezbollah fired rockets and artillery in retaliation.[^cite_note-162] During the clashes, two Israeli soldiers and Lt. Col [Alim Abdallah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alim_Abdallah), Deputy Commander of the IDF's 300th Brigade, were killed and another three were wounded.[^cite_note-INN12-36]

On 11 October, Hezbollah fired anti-tank missiles at an IDF position and claimed to have produced casualties. In response, the IDF shelled the area where the attack was launched.[^cite_note-163] The Lebanese-Italian hospital in [Tyre](30796.md) admitted three injured civilians.[^cite_note-164] The IDF ordered residents of northern Israel to seek shelter following reports of drones being launched from southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-165] A [Patriot missile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriot_missile) was launched to intercept a suspicious projectile, after which the IDF found that the object in question was not a drone.[^cite_note-166] Warning sirens were activated across northern Israel after reports emerged that up to 20 infiltrators on paragliders had entered Israeli territory from Lebanon before the IDF dismissed the report as a [false alarm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_alarm).[^cite_note-167]

Hezbollah deputy chief Sheikh [Naim Qassem](28105310.md) said "when time comes for any action, we will carry it out," stating that Hezbollah was ready and would contribute to confrontations against Israel according to its own plan.[^cite_note-168] The IDF fired artillery, into southern Lebanon following an explosion that caused minor damage to a section of the Israel–Lebanon border wall near the kibbutz of [Hanita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanita).[^cite_note-169]

The IDF released footage of a drone attack which, according to them, killed three infiltrators from Lebanon near [Margaliot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margaliot) who were members of Hamas.[^cite_note-170] One of them was acknowledged by Hezbollah as one of their members. In the afternoon, Hezbollah fired 50 mortar shells and six anti-tank missiles towards five Israeli outposts in the Shebaa Farms.[^cite_note-Khaleej-Times-20231014-171] Further IDF shelling killed two civilians in a Shebaa village; video and photographic evidence showed usage of [phosphorus bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phosphorus_bombs).[^cite_note-172]

&#x20;On 15 October, Hezbollah launched five anti-tank missiles towards northern Israel killing one civilian and injuring three others in [Shtula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shtula).[^cite_note-173][^cite_note-174] [UNIFIL](787766.md) said that their headquarters in [Naqoura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naqoura) in southern Lebanon was hit by rocket fire with no casualties reported.[^cite_note-175] Lieutenant Amitai Granot, commander of the 75th Battalion of the IDF's [Golan Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golani_Brigade) and son of [Rabbi Tamir Granot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamir_Granot), was killed in a missile attack on an IDF post bordering Lebanon.[^cite_note-inn20231015-32][^cite_note-jpost20231015-33]



A protester holding up a sign of Issam Abdallah during [mass demonstrations in London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_in_the_United_Kingdom)

On 13 October, while a group of [Reuters](18998750.md), [AFP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agence_France_Presse) and [Al Jazeera](30204052.md) journalists were transmitting a live video feed of an IDF outpost in [Aalma ech Chaab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aalma_ech_Chaab), two tank rounds fired directly hit the group. The first killed Reuters photojournalist [Issam Abdallah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Issam_Abdallah). The second strike was much more powerful and ignited the Al Jazeera vehicle, a white [Toyota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toyota), which Al Jazeera journalists Carmen Joukhadar and Elie Brakhya, as well as their AFP colleague Dylan Collins were standing next to.[^cite_note-reuters20231207-176] Reuters photographer Christina Assi was also critically injured.[^cite_note-177][^cite_note-Neumann-2023-178] The [Lebanese Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Army) has said the IDF fired the missile that killed Abdallah. Another Reuters reporter at the scene said Abdallah was killed by projectiles fired from the direction of Israel.[^cite_note-Obituary-2023-179] His last post on [Instagram](31591547.md), posted a week before he was killed, was a photograph of [Shireen Abu Akleh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shireen_Abu_Akleh), a Palestinian journalist for [Al Jazeera Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Jazeera_Arabic) who had been killed by Israel in 2022.[^cite_note-180][^cite_note-apnews20231014-181]

On 16 October, [Amnesty International](18947898.md) reported that the IDF fired [white phosphorus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_phosphorus_munitions) shells into Dhayra, hospitalizing nine civilians and setting fire to civilian objects.[^cite_note-182] Aya Majzoub, Amnesty International's Deputy Regional Director for the Middle East and North Africa, described the attack as a violation of international law that needed to be investigated as a war crime, and that it "seriously endangered the lives of civilians, many of whom were hospitalized and displaced, and whose homes and cars caught fire".[^cite_note-AmnestyLebanonWP-183]

Lebanese state media reported that Dhayra and other areas along the western section of the border came under "continuous" bombardment overnight.[^cite_note-184] In the early morning it was reported that multiple people were suffering from symptoms of suffocation after the IDF allegedly fired white phosphorus shells on the village.[^cite_note-185] Three people were injured after an anti-tank missile from Lebanon landed in the Israeli town of [Metula](2599433.md).[^cite_note-186] Hezbollah announced that five of its members were killed on the same day but it was unclear if any had involvement in the border infiltration.[^cite_note-187]

On 19 October, the [Lebanese Armed Forces](17779.md) said that one person was killed and another injured after a group of seven Iranian journalists were targeted with machine guns by Israel, although Iranian state media denied the claim and said that all its journalists were "alive and healthy".[^cite_note-The-New-Arab-2023-188] UNIFIL peacekeepers said that one person was killed when civilians were caught in a cross-fire at the border. The Lebanese Army requested assistance by UNIFIL to de-escalate the situation, and Israel was asked to suspend fire to allow the rescue operation to proceed.[^cite_note-189][^cite_note-The-New-Arab-2023-188] Israel started evacuating the settlements located close to the border with Lebanon in October 2023 with more than 60 thousand evacuated by April 2024.[^cite_note-DavarDisplaced-190][^cite_note-191]

In the early afternoon of 21 October, several rockets were fired from Lebanon toward the Shebaa Farms; there were no injuries. The IDF conducted a drone strike on the team of militants that launched the rockets.[^cite_note-192] A short while later, anti-tank guided missiles were fired from Lebanon toward Margaliot and [Hanita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanita); two foreign workers were injured. The IDF conducted airstrikes against the missile teams.[^cite_note-193] In the evening, another anti-tank guided missile was fired from Lebanon toward [Bar'am](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bar'am). One IDF soldier was seriously injured and two others suffered minor injuries. The IDF responded with several airstrikes in southern Lebanon, some of which targeted other missile teams preparing attacks.[^cite_note-194]

The IDF conducted airstrikes against two Syrian military positions on 24 October in southwestern Syria, marking the first time the IDF publicly targeted the Syrian military since the Israel–Gaza war began.[^cite_note-iran-update-october-25-2023-29] Hezbollah attacked 19 IDF military sites with missiles and artillery shells[^cite_note-iran-update-october-29-2023-195] and fired one-way attack drones at an IDF position for the first time since the conflict began.[^cite_note-iran-update-november-2-2023-196]

#### November 2023



Hezbollah supporters attend a [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md) speech in Beirut, 3 November 2023

In his widely awaited first speech since the start of the war in Gaza on 3 November, Hezbollah leader [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md) said the presence of U.S. warships in the Mediterranean "doesn't scare us".[^cite_note-197][^cite_note-198] On 5 November, Hezbollah shot down an Israeli [Elbit Hermes 450](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elbit_Hermes_450) drone over [Nabatieh](43794798.md),[^cite_note-199] with wreckage falling over houses in the towns of [Zabdin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zabdin) and [Harouf](36320057.md).[^cite_note-200] One Israeli civilian was killed when anti-tank missiles hit the [Yiftah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yiftah) kibbutz.[^cite_note-All-Israel-News-2023-201]

Four people were reportedly injured after an Israeli bombing that hit two ambulances.[^cite_note-macaubusiness20231105-202] Later, an Israeli airstrike hit two civilian cars in Lebanon between the towns of [Aynata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aynata) and [Aitaroun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aitaroun), killing one woman, three of her granddaughters between the ages of 10 and 14, and severely injured her daughter.[^cite_note-203] In response, Hezbollah fired at [Kiryat Shmona](323369.md),[^cite_note-204] killing an Israeli civilian.[^cite_note-205]

The [Al-Qassam Brigades](1703333.md) took responsibility for firing 16 rockets from Lebanon targeting areas south of [Haifa](55606.md).[^cite_note-206] Meanwhile, Israel reported at least 30 rockets being fired in which the IDF fired back at the sources. Hezbollah and Al-Qassam brigades also conducted four cross-border attacks into northern Israel.[^cite_note-iran-update-october-29-2023-195] Hezbollah deputy general [Naim Qassem](28105310.md) said that the group could be forced into wider conflict over Israeli attacks in Gaza.[^cite_note-207]

On 10 November, Hezbollah launched anti-tank missiles into an IDF post in [Manara](3577562.md) which injured three soldiers. The IDF attacked the sources of fire in response.[^cite_note-208] Hezbollah conducted three drone attacks into northern Israel targeting IDF positions and civilians.[^cite_note-209] One drone was intercepted while two others landed on Israeli territory.[^cite_note-210] Seven Hezbollah members were killed during the clashes. The IDF shelled [Meiss Ej Jabal Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meiss_Ej_Jabal_Hospital), injuring a doctor. Lebanon's Ministry of Public Health condemned the attack, saying that "Israeli authorities were fully responsible for this unjustifiable act, which would have led to catastrophic results", and called for an investigation.[^cite_note-arabnews20231111-211]

The [Amal Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amal_Movement), an ally of Hezbollah, announced that a fighter was killed in a missile attack in the village of [Rab El Thalathine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rab_El_Thalathine) which also wounded two other members on 11 November.[^cite_note-212] These were the first casualties from the group since it joined the fighting.[^cite_note-213] On 12 November, Hezbollah anti-tank missile and mortar attacks killed an employee of the [Israel Electric Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Electric_Corporation) who was conducting repair work and injured 21 other Israelis, including seven IDF members and six of the fatality's colleagues.[^cite_note-The-Jerusalem-Post-20231112-214][^cite_note-barrons20231113-215] Hezbollah also struck an IDF bulldozer in a separate attack. The IDF said it had launched a drone strike at a militant cell that tried to launch antitank missiles near Metula.[^cite_note-apnov12-216] Further clashes also killed one Hezbollah member.[^cite_note-Khaleej-Times-20231014-171]

Following a Hezbollah strike on 13 November, the IDF responded with heavy shelling across southern Lebanon which reportedly killed two people, according to a first-responder organisation affiliated to the Hezbollah-allied Amal Movement.[^cite_note-217] Unidentified fighters fired anti-tank guided missiles that injured two Israelis near [Netu'a](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netu'a).[^cite_note-218] An Israeli rocket struck near journalists in [Yaroun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yaroun), Lebanon, no casualties were reported.[^cite_note-219] Hezbollah condemned the attack, which happened while the journalists were on a public tour in the town.[^cite_note-220]

On 16 November, Hezbollah conducted eight anti-tank missiles targeting Israeli forces and military infrastructure.[^cite_note-221] In the afternoon, Hezbollah attacked numerous towns near the border and targeted military gatherings in Shtula and Hadab Yaron.[^cite_note-222][^cite_note-223] The IDF responded heavily in southern Lebanon and Israeli warplanes raided Hezbollah targets.[^cite_note-224] Hezbollah announced that two of its members were killed.[^cite_note-225]

Four days later, the IDF base of [Biranit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biranit) suffered heavy damage from a Hezbollah barrage using Burkan rockets.[^cite_note-226] IDF fighter jets struck numerous Hezbollah military targets, and soldiers struck a militant cell near Metula.[^cite_note-227] The historically significant St. George church was heavily damaged in Yaroun after it was shelled by the IDF.[^cite_note-228] The house of Amal Movement MP [Kabalan Kabalan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabalan_Kabalan?action=edit\&redlink=1) was also hit with rocket fire.[^cite_note-229] Hezbollah claimed an attack on the IDF's [91st Division](5475696.md) barracks at Baranit.[^cite_note-Khaleej-Times-20231014-171]

On 21 November, an IDF airstrike in [Kafr Kila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kafr_Kila,_Lebanon) killed an elderly woman and injured her granddaughter.[^cite_note-230] Another team of journalists were targeted in an IDF strike near [Tayr Harfa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tayr_Harfa) which killed three people, including two [Al Mayadeen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Mayadeen) journalists, a reporter and a photojournalist, and a guide.[^cite_note-231] On the same day, four members of the Al-Qassam Brigades were killed after an IDF strike on a car near [Chaaitiyeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaaitiyeh?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-232] A Hezbollah member was also killed in a separate attack in [Khiam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khiam).[^cite_note-233]

Hezbollah told Al Jazeera that it would "respect" the [temporary ceasefire deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Gaza_war_ceasefire) between Israel and Hamas reached on 24 November 2023.[^cite_note-234] Subsequently, Hezbollah decreased its military operations briefly which also prompted the IDF to decrease its shelling of targets in southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-235] Many displaced civilians reportedly returned to their homes amid the calm.[^cite_note-236] Still, Hezbollah claimed 23 other attacks into northern Israel until the breaking of the ceasefire on 30 November.[^cite_note-iran-update-november-24-2023-237]

On 25 November, a UNIFIL patrol unit was hit by IDF gunfire in the vicinity of Aitaroun where there were no casualties. UNIFIL condemned the incident and called on parties to be reminded "of their obligations to protect peacekeepers and avoid putting the men and women who are working to restore stability at risk."[^cite_note-238]

#### December 2023

On 1 December, Hezbollah claimed five attacks on the Israel–Lebanon border.[^cite_note-239] The IDF shelled [Hula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hula,_Lebanon), killing two civilians,[^cite_note-240] and the village of [Jebbayn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jebbayn), killing an additional person.[^cite_note-241] The IDF also struck a Hezbollah site and a Hezbollah cell preparing to carry out an attack near Malkia. Hezbollah announced the death of one of their members, presumably from one of the IDF strikes.[^cite_note-242] The next day, Hezbollah fired several rockets at Israeli army posts along the border. Israel responded with airstrikes and artillery shelling against Hezbollah sites.[^cite_note-243] Hezbollah stated that one of its fighters were killed.[^cite_note-244]

On 3 December, Hezbollah fired one anti-tank guided missile targeting an IDF base in [Beit Hillel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Hillel), injuring 11 Israelis and [Global Affairs Canada](256258.md) announced that a Canadian citizen was killed in Lebanon.[^cite_note-245][^cite_note-iran-update-december-3-2023-246] On 4 December, Hamas announced the creation of a new unit in Lebanon named the "Al-Aqsa Flood Vanguards" and called on "the youth and men of our people to join the vanguard resistance fighters and take part in shaping the future and liberating Jerusalem and the al-Aqsa Mosque."[^cite_note-Al-Aqsa-28] This created a negative reaction by many Lebanese politicians as they said that it would be a threat to Lebanon's sovereignty.[^cite_note-Al-Aqsa-28]

One Lebanese soldier was killed and three others were injured by an Israeli attack on a Lebanese Army base in [Odaisseh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odaisseh).[^cite_note-thenationalnews20231205-247] The IDF later apologized for the incident, saying that it would investigate.[^cite_note-248] A farm worker from Syria, was killed in an Israeli artillery attack on a poultry farm near [Arnoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arnoun) that also injured two of his relatives.[^cite_note-thenationalnews20231205-247] Israeli [defense minister](3226446.md) Yoav Gallant met with mayors and local council heads in [Nahariya](323332.md), northern Israel to discuss the threat of Hezbollah to northern residents. Gallant said that if diplomacy fails, Israel will use its military to force Hezbollah north of the [Litani River](1920925.md).[^cite_note-iran-update-december-6-2023-249]

On 11 December, the mayor of the southern Lebanese village of [Taybeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taybeh_\(Marjaayoun\)) was killed in an Israeli airstrike.[^cite_note-250] Two other Hezbollah fighters were killed by Israeli airstrikes and artillery shelling in Aitaroun, and three civilians were injured. Other airstrikes were carried out by the IAF, destroying five houses and damaging 17 others.[^cite_note-The-New-Arab-2023-188] Four batches of missiles fired from Lebanon towards northern Israel were intercepted by the [Iron Dome](18342427.md) defense system, while the Islamic Resistance claimed it attacked several Israeli sites. According to Israeli media, three soldiers were injured.[^cite_note-The-New-Arab-2023-188] Hezbollah and other militias attacked nine Israeli towns and military positions.[^cite_note-iran-update-december-11-2023-251] Israeli war cabinet minister and former defense minister [Benny Gantz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benny_Gantz) discussed security in northern Israel in a phone call with US secretary of state [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken). Gantz said he conveyed that Hezbollah's heightened aggression and attacks mean that Israel must "remove" the threat from northern Israel. Gantz's statement singled out the Lebanese state, not just Hezbollah, and called for international pressure on Lebanon to stop attacks on its southern border.[^cite_note-The-New-Arab-2023-188]

The [Syrian Social Nationalist Party in Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Social_Nationalist_Party_in_Lebanon) announced the death of one member of their military wing, the [Eagles of the Whirlwind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eagles_of_the_Whirlwind), on 15 December.[^cite_note-nna-leb-20231212-8] The IDF said that one in five rockets launched by Hezbollah had landed in Lebanese territory and published an infographic showing failed rocket launches into Israel.[^cite_note-252] On 16 December, a soldier from the IDF's 129th Battalion was killed by a Hezbollah drone strike near Margaliot, which also caused a fire in a building. Two other soldiers were injured. Another drone was shot down by the IDF, which also responded to the infiltrations by shelling targets. The IAF later hit several Hezbollah targets inside Lebanon, including rocket launch sites and military infrastructure.[^cite_note-jpost20231216-37] On 22 December, two IDF soldiers of the [188th Armored Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/188th_Armored_Brigade) were killed by a Hezbollah rocket attack in the Shtula area.[^cite_note-253]

On the morning of 23 December, IDF aircraft bombed a house in Kfar Kila and intense artillery fire also took place on the outskirts of [Deir Mimas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deir_Mimas)[^cite_note-254] The IDF conducted a raid near a UNIFL center along the [Khardali River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khardali_river?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-255] An [Al-Manar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Manar) cameraman was injured in the eye after an IDF attack on a road in the al-Khardali area where correspondents of [MTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_\(Lebanon\)) and the state-owned [National News Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_National_News_Agency) were also passing.[^cite_note-Naharnet-2023-256] Hezbollah announced that two of their members were killed on that day.[^cite_note-Naharnet-2023-256] On 24 December, senior [IRGC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IRGC) general [Razi Mousavi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Razi_Mousavi) was killed by an Israeli airstrike in [Damascus](8914.md).[^cite_note-257] On 26 December, a Hezbollah anti-tank missile hit St. Mary's Greek Orthodox Church in [Iqrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iqrit), northern Israel.[^cite_note-258] A civilian suffered serious injuries, and when IDF responders arrived at the scene, another missile was fired, injuring nine soldiers.[^cite_note-259] On 30 December, Israeli airstrikes targeted an Iranian arms shipment in the Syrian border city of [Abu Kamal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Kamal), killing 25 militiamen.[^cite_note-260]

#### January 2024

On 2 January, Israel conducted an airstrike in the [Dahieh](6082788.md) neighborhood of [Beirut](37428.md), resulting in the [assassination of Saleh al-Arouri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Saleh_al-Arouri), the deputy chairman of the Hamas political bureau, and the death of six other members.[^cite_note-middleeasteye2jan-261] Al-Arouri was also responsible for the expansion of Hamas's activities in the [Israeli-occupied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli-occupied) [West Bank](33209.md), including attacks on Israelis.[^cite_note-The-Jerusalem-Post-20240103-262][^cite_note-Kampeas-2024-263] The assassination occurred one day before Hezbollah commemorated the fourth anniversary of the [assassination](62726668.md) of senior Iranian military commander [Qasem Soleimani](16805217.md).[^cite_note-264] On 6 January, Hezbollah launched approximately 40 rockets into northern Israel, describing it as an "initial response" to al-Arouri's killing.[^cite_note-265] According to Israel, the rockets targeted a [Air traffic Control Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_traffic_Control_Commend_\(Israel\)) near [Mount Meron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Meron), causing significant damage to it.[^cite_note-266]

On 8 January, Israel assassinated [Wissam al-Tawil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wissam_al-Tawil), the deputy commander of Hezbollah's [Redwan Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redwan_Force), whom it accused of carrying out the attack on Meron airbase two days earlier.[^cite_note-267] In response to al-Tawil's killing, Hezbollah launched a drone attack on Israel's [Northern Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Command_\(Israel\)) headquarters in [Safed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safed) on the following day, situated approximately 20 kilometres (12 mi) from the border.[^cite_note-268] [Ali Hussein Barji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Hussein_Barji), the commander of Hezbollah's aerial forces in southern Lebanon who was reportedly responsible for the attack, was killed by an airstrike, according to Israel, alongside three other Hezbollah members, in [Khirbet Selm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khirbet_Selm) shortly before al-Tawil's funeral began.[^cite_note-269][^cite_note-270] However, Hezbollah denied that Barji was killed.[^cite_note-271]

On 11 January, two Hezbollah-linked search-and-rescue personnel were killed and an unspecified number of civilians were wounded during an Israeli air raid on the town of [Hanine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanine).[^cite_note-272] The IDF said that they killed four militants that infiltrated the Israeli border through Shebaa while an IDF unit was patrolling nearby on 14 January. Five IDF soldiers were wounded.[^cite_note-273] A group calling itself the "Islamic Glory Brigades" later claimed responsibility for the attack and announced that 3 of their members were killed and 2 were able to escape.[^cite_note-274] The same day, two Israeli civilians; a man and his 70-year-old mother, were killed by Hezbollah anti-tank missiles in [Kfar Yuval](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Yuval).[^cite_note-275]

On 20 January, [Brigadier General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brigadier_General) [Sadegh Omidzadeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sadegh_Omidzadeh), an intelligence officer with the IRGC's [Quds Force](4028355.md) in Syria, along with four other IRGC officers, were reportedly killed in an [Israeli airstrike in Damascus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_airstrike_on_the_Iranian_consulate_in_Damascus).[^cite_note-276][^cite_note-277] According to the Syrian Observatory for Human Rights, an Israeli missile strike targeted a four-story building in the [Mezzeh](21089456.md) district. The attack resulted in the death of thirteen individuals,[^cite_note-278] including five Iranians, and the complete destruction of the building where leaders aligned with Iran were having a meeting.[^cite_note-syriahr20jan-279] The next day, in southern Lebanon, Sky News Arabic reported that Fadi Suleiman, a senior field commander of Hezbollah, survived an Israeli attack near a military checkpoint in [Kafra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kafra,_Lebanon). His bodyguard was killed in the same attack.[^cite_note-280]

#### February 2024

Between 3 and 5 February, five Amal movement fighters were reported killed: two in airstrikes in [Blida](42906975.md) and three in [Bayt Lif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayt_Lif).[^cite_note-281][^cite_note-282] On 8 February, the IDF conducted a drone strike on a car in Nabatieh. Israeli media said a regional Hezbollah commander named Abbas al-Dabs was assassinated in the attack.[^cite_note-283] A day before, two Hezbollah members, including al-Dabs, were named by Israeli intelligence as reportedly working alongside IRGC officers on building Iranian air defenses in Syria.[^cite_note-284]

On 10 February, an Israeli drone struck a car near Sidon, killing at least two people and wounding two others. An Israeli security source said the target of the strike was Hamas official Basel Saleh, who was "injured to an unknown extent". Saleh was responsible for enlisting new Hamas recruits in Gaza and the West Bank.[^cite_note-285] On 12 February, another assassination attempt took place targeting Hezbollah official Mohammed Abd al-Rasoul Alawiya in his car in Bint Jbeil.[^cite_note-286]

On 14 February, in the deadliest day of fighting,[^cite_note-287] a barrage of eleven rockets fired from Lebanon struck Safed and an army base in northern Israel, killing an Israeli soldier and injuring eight others. Israel responded with widespread airstrikes against targets belonging to Hezbollah infrastructure in Jabal al-Braij, Kfar Houneh, Kafr Dunin, Aadchit and Souaneh, killing a total of ten people.[^cite_note-288] In Nabatieh, an attack on a residential building killed seven members of a family, including a child. Another attack in the town of al-Suwana killed a woman and her two children.[^cite_note-289] The IDF said that it killed a senior commander of the elite [Redwan Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redwan_Force) unit along with a deputy and another fighter in Nabatieh which was confirmed by Hezbollah the next day.[^cite_note-290][^cite_note-291] A total of seven Hezbollah members were announced dead from the attacks.[^cite_note-292]

In response to the launch of a UAV toward the Lower Galilee from Lebanese territory on 19 February, Israeli jets targeted Hezbollah militants in Marwahin, two weapons storage facilities near the city of Sidon, and Hezbollah infrastructure in the Meiss El Jabal and al-Adisa areas of Lebanon.[^cite_note-293]

On 21 February, an Israeli airstrike on a residential area of Damascus near a fortified compound used by Syrian security forces killed two civilians.[^cite_note-294] On the same day, an Israeli airstrike on the village of Majdal Zoun killed two more civilians, a mother and her child.[^cite_note-295] In addition, Israeli forces struck a Hezbollah observation post in Ramyeh and shelled an area near Aitaroun with artillery to "remove threats." Later on Israeli jets attacked an observation post in Khaim, a military post in Zibqin—from which a missile was fired at Shlomi—and other Hezbollah infrastructure.[^cite_note-296]

On 22 February, after a projectile from Lebanon hit a home in the border town of Yuval, an Israeli drone struck an apartment building in Kfar Remen, killing at least two Hezbollah fighters and wounding three others.[^cite_note-297] The next day, on 23 February, an Israeli strike on a Hezbollah-linked clinic in Blida killed two civilian paramedics and one Hezbollah fighter and injured an unknown number of people.[^cite_note-298]

On 24 February, sirens sounded in Arab el Aramsha, Hanita, Shebaa Farms, and Kiryat Shmona in northern Israel and the Golan Heights due to rocket fire. In response, the IDF struck numerous sites across the areas of Rab El Thalathine, Ayta ash Shab, and Blida. Later an Israeli drone identified a Hezbollah cell entering a military compound in southern Lebanon, leading to an Israeli airstrike on the compound. After the attack, secondary explosions took place, implying the presence of weapons in the building. In addition, fighter jets attacked a Redwan unit operational headquarters near the village of Baraachit, as well as launch posts in the area of Jabal Blat, while Israeli artillery struck to neutralize a threat in the area of [Rachaya Al Foukhar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rachaya_Al_Foukhar).[^cite_note-299]

On 26 February, after Hezbollah shot down a [Hermes 450](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hermes_450) drone with a surface-to-air missile, Israel launched its first attacks into eastern Lebanon since the conflict began, targeting Hezbollah air defense sites and killing two Hezbollah members near [Baalbek](58207.md).[^cite_note-300][^cite_note-301] Hezbollah said it fired 60 Katyusha rockets at an IDF headquarters in response to the attack.[^cite_note-302] The IDF eliminated a commander in Hezbollah's Nasser Unit who was responsible for multiple attacks into Israel while driving in his car in Majadel.[^cite_note-303]

#### March 2024

On 4 March, a Hezbollah anti-tank missile strike on Margaliot resulted in seven injuries as well as the death of an Indian national.[^cite_note-304] The next day, an Israeli airstrike on Hula, in retaliation for the previous day's strike on Margaliot, killed three Lebanese citizens, a married couple and their child.[^cite_note-arabnews-2024-03-05-305] Amongst the three killed, Hezbollah announced two as their members.[^cite_note-306][^cite_note-307] On 9 March, five people, including three Hezbollah members, were killed and at least nine others were wounded in an IDF strike on a house in [Khirbet Selm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khirbet_Selm).[^cite_note-308] In retaliation, the next day Hezbollah fired 37 Katyusha rockets at the [Mount Meron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Meron) in northern Israel; no injuries were reported.[^cite_note-309][^cite_note-310]



IDF soldiers training as part of the newly formed [Mountain Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HeHarim_Brigade) in northern Israel

On 12 March, Israeli airstrikes on the town of [al-Nabi Shayth](6016255.md) in the [Beqaa Valley](2162385.md) resulted in six injuries and the death of two Hezbollah members.[^cite_note-311] On 13 March, an Israeli airstrike on a road near Lebanon's [Rashidieh refugee camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashidieh) for Palestinians killed a civilian and Hadi Ali Mustafa, the leader of Hamas forces in Lebanon, and injured two others.[^cite_note-312][^cite_note-313] A week later, two Syrian teenagers (aged 16 and 17) were handed over to Lebanese authorities for allegedly spying for Israel. It was reported that they confessed to have been given an equivalent of US\$11 each by a local mosque servant to unknowingly plant a tracking device in Mustafa's car.[^cite_note-314]

On 27 March, the IDF reported that its airstrike on a paramedic center affiliated with the group in [Hebbariye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebbariye) killed seven people, whom were reportedly militants. The report was denied by Hezbollah, which said that the casualties were actually rescuers.[^cite_note-BBC-News-20240327-315] The victims were later identified as volunteers, and Lebanon's Ministry of Public Health condemned the strike. In response to the attack, Hezbollah launched around thirty rockets towards northern Israel, killing a factory worker in Kiryat Shmona and lightly wounding another.[^cite_note-Al-Jazeera-20240327-316][^cite_note-317] After sunset, Israeli airstrikes were reported in [Tayr Harfa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tayr_Harfa) and Naqoura, which each killed five and four people respectively. The strikes killed three paramedics; two from the Islamic Health Society and another from the Amal Movement-affiliated Islamic Risala Scout Association. A local commander of the Amal Movement was also killed, along with at least two Hezbollah members.[^cite_note-AP-News-2024-318]

On 29 March, an Israeli airstrike targeting [Aleppo International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleppo_International_Airport) killed 38 Syrian soldiers, seven Hezbollah fighters and seven militiamen,[^cite_note-cassyr2024-73] in what became the deadliest Israeli strike on Syria in the past three years.[^cite_note-319] Another airstrike in Bazouriyeh, Lebanon killed Ali Abed Akhsan Naim, the deputy commander of Hezbollah's rocket and missiles unit who the IDF said was responsible for planning attacks against Israeli civilians.[^cite_note-320] On 30 March, Reuters reported that an Israeli strike had injured three UN observers and a translator who were monitoring hostilities along the Blue Line.[^cite_note-321] An investigation by the Lebanese Army determined that the explosion was caused by a landmine, which Israel said was planted by Hezbollah.[^cite_note-322] The IDF struck a centre of the Lebanese Ambulance Association at the end of March killing seven paramedics.[^cite_note-323]

### Initial Iranian involvement (April – July 2024)

See also: [Israeli airstrike on the Iranian consulate in Damascus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_airstrike_on_the_Iranian_consulate_in_Damascus) and [April 2024 Iranian strikes on Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_2024_Iranian_strikes_on_Israel)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of the Israel–Hezbollah conflict (1 April – 26 July 2024)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Israel%E2%80%93Hezbollah_conflict_\(1_April_%E2%80%93_26_July_2024\)).

On 1 April, an Israeli airstrike targeted an Iranian consulate annex building adjacent to the Iranian embassy in Damascus, Syria. It killed eight people, including a senior Quds Force commander of the IRGC, Brigadier General [Mohammad Reza Zahedi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Reza_Zahedi).[^cite_note-324] The others fatalities included five members of the Islamic Revolutionary Guards Corps and two Iranian advisors.[^cite_note-325] On 8 April, Israeli officials confirmed that Hezbollah fighters shot down a Hermes 900 drone, the first of its type lost during the conflict.[^cite_note-326] On 13 April, the [Islamic Revolutionary Guards Corps](1285472.md) (IRGC), a branch of the [Iranian military](14305018.md), in collaboration with the [Popular Mobilization Forces](45671374.md) in [Iraq](7515928.md), Hezbollah, and the [Houthis](17240332.md) in [Yemen](350939.md), launched [attacks against Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_2024_Iranian_strikes_against_Israel) with [drones](58900.md), [cruise missiles](6590.md), and [ballistic missiles](38025.md).[^cite_note-Washington_Post-20240414-327]

On 16 April, an Israeli airstrike on two cars near the town of [Ain Ebel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ain_Ebel), in southern Lebanon, killed two Hezbollah members, including regional commander Ismail Baz, and one civilian.[^cite_note-reuters20240416-143] The next day, fourteen Israeli soldiers and four civilians were wounded by drones attack in northern Israel. One IDF officer died of his wounds later that week.[^cite_note-C21IDF-328][^cite_note-329] On 23 April, an Israeli drone strike deep into Lebanon killed an engineer working for Hezbollah's air defense forces as he was traveling in a vehicle. The strike hit the Abu al-Aswad area near the coastal city of Tyre, some 35 kilometers (22 miles) from the border. The fighter's vehicle was completely burnt out. The IDF said it had killed "two significant terrorists in Hezbollah's aerial unit", the second being a senior member of the elite [Redwan Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redwan_Force), Muhammad Attiya.[^cite_note-330] In response to the attacks, Hezbollah made its deepest incursion into Israeli territory at the time by launching drone attacks into the city of [Acre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acre,_Israel) targeting two IDF bases.[^cite_note-331]

On 5 May, an Israel airstrike on a house in the Lebanese village of Meiss Ej Jabal killed four civilians. In retaliation, Hezbollah fired dozens of rockets at the Israeli town of Kiryat Shmona.[^cite_note-332] On 6 May, two reservist Israeli soldiers of the [98th Paratroopers Division](12871166.md)'s 655th Battalion were killed by a Hezbollah drone attack near [Metula](2599433.md), northern Israel, bringing the IDF death toll to 18.[^cite_note-333] Hezbollah also launched 30 rockets at the Golan Heights, damaging houses in [Kidmat Tzvi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kidmat_Tzvi).[^cite_note-334] The IDF struck 15 Radwan targets in [al-Lwaiza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Lwaiza), southern Lebanon,[^cite_note-335] and a Hezbollah compound in Sefri, near Baalbek. Lebanese media said that the strike destroyed a factory and injured three people.[^cite_note-The-Jerusalem-Post-20240506-336][^cite_note-337]

On 8 May, Israeli airstrikes in southern Lebanon killed two Hezbollah and three PIJ members.[^cite_note-338] The next day an Israeli airstrike on a car near the village of [Bafliyeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bafliyeh?action=edit\&redlink=1) killed four Hezbollah members.[^cite_note-339] On 10 May, Israeli airstrikes on two villages in the [Marjeyoun District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marjeyoun_District) killed a Hezbollah member in [Aedsheet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aedsheet) and a PIJ member in Blida, respectively. On the same day, another Israeli airstrike on the town of Tayr Harfa killed two civilians who were carrying out repairs on a local cell tower.[^cite_note-340]

On 14 May, an Israeli airstrike on a house in the village of Meiss Ej Jabal killed a member of Hezbollah and injured one other person. Following this, a Hezbollah anti-tank missile strike killed an Israeli civilian and wounded five IDF personnel in the kibbutz of [Adamit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adamit).[^cite_note-341] At night, an Israeli drone strike on a car in Tyre killed two people including Hussein Makki, a Hezbollah field commander.[^cite_note-342] In response to the commander's killing, Hezbollah fired 60 rockets at [Mount Meron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Meron) air traffic control base causing minor damage. They also fired at least one heavy rocket towards the Biranit army base.[^cite_note-The-Times-of-Israel-20240515-343] One IDF soldier was killed and another five were wounded.[^cite_note-344]

On 15 May, a rocket launched by the Lebanese branch of Hamas fell short of the intended Israeli target, instead landing on the Lebanese side of the border, killing a Syrian immigrant and wounding four others. A Hezbollah drone crashed in the [Lower Galilee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lower_Galilee) for the first time since the start of the conflict. Hezbollah said it launched several drones targeting a surveillance system at an army base near [Golani Junction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golani_Junction), west of [Tiberias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiberias).[^cite_note-345][^cite_note-346] The IDF confirmed that two drones were fired at the Tal Shamayim base, with one being intercepted and the other damaging the [Sky Dew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_Dew) aerostat.[^cite_note-347][^cite_note-348] Later that day, an Israeli airstrike on a car near Tyre killed one Hezbollah member and wounded two other people.[^cite_note-349] The next day, two other Hezbollah members were in another airstrike on another car near in the same area.[^cite_note-350] In retaliation, Hezbollah launched a drone strike on Israeli positions in Metula, wounding three IDF soldiers.

On 21 May, an Israeli airstrike near Tyre killed a high-ranking officer in Hezbollah's coastal missile unit. That night, an Israeli airstrike on a car in the Beqaa Valley killed two members of the Islamic Group. Targeted assassinations continued the same week with an Israeli airstrike on a car in the village of [Kfar Dajjal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Dajjal?action=edit\&redlink=1) which killed a Hezbollah member and seriously wounded three children in a nearby school bus. In response, Lebanon issued a formal complaint to the United Nations Security Council.[^cite_note-351]

On 25 May, an Israeli airstrike on a truck near the Syrian town of [al-Qusayr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qusayr,_Syria) killed two Hezbollah members.[^cite_note-352] Later that day, a car bombing in Damascus killed a Syrian Army officer with ties to Hezbollah.[^cite_note-353] On 26 May, an Israeli airstrike on a house in Aitaroun killed two Hezbollah members. On the same day, another Hezbollah member was killed, as was another person, following an airstrike on a motorcycle in Naqoura. Later, an airstrike on another motorcycle in Ayta ash Shab killed two civilians. An airstrike on a third motorcycle, in Hula, killed two Hezbollah members and wounded two other people. Two civilians were killed in strikes on the village of Yaroun.[^cite_note-354]

On 1 June, an Israeli airstrike on a house in [Aadloun](18434545.md) killed a civilian and wounded four others. Later that evening, two Israeli airstrikes on a house in [Ain Qana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ain_Qana) killed a Hezbollah member and wounded another person.[^cite_note-355] The next day, an Israeli airstrike on a motorcycle in Hula killed two civilians.[^cite_note-356] On 3 June, a pair of Israeli airstrikes on Naqoura killed two Hezbollah members. Another airstrike, this time on a car near the village of [Kauthariyet El Rez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kauthariyet_El_Rez?action=edit\&redlink=1), killed another Hezbollah member.[^cite_note-357] In Syria, an Israeli airstrike on a copper plant near [Aleppo](159244.md) killed 16 members of Iran-backed militias.[^cite_note-Reuters-20240602-358] Hezbollah said that it fired drones at the IDF's headquarters in the Galilee.[^cite_note-359] It also claimed a drone attack in Metula.[^cite_note-The-Times-of-Israel-20240603-360] The IDF confirmed that two drones crashed in northern Israel while a third was intercepted.[^cite_note-Reuters-20240603-361] Acting [Minister of Foreign Affairs](1166971.md) of Iran made his first overseas visit as foreign minister to [Lebanon](17771.md),[^cite_note-362] during which he confirmed that the Iranian government was engaged in negotiations with the United States hosted by Oman.[^cite_note-363] Forest fires in northern Israel were erupted the entire week which reportedly resulted in 2,500 acres of land burnt and the hospitalization of six Israeli soldiers and five civilians due to smoke inhalation.[^cite_note-wildfires-in-northern-israel-ignited-by-hezbollah-rockets-364][^cite_note-365]

On 11 June, an Israeli strike on a command and control center in [Jwaya](38389424.md) killed senior Hezbollah commander [Taleb Abdallah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taleb_Abdallah?action=edit\&redlink=1) and three other militants. The next day, in response to Taleb's killing,[^cite_note-Reuters-20240611-366] Hezbollah launched a barrage of about 90 rockets into Israel targeting an IDF factory, military headquarters in Ein Zeitim and [Ami'ad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ami'ad), and an air surveillance station in Meron.[^cite_note-Reuters-20240611-366] It later fired another 70 at the Mount Meron area, and then ten more at Zar'it, bringing the total number of launches to 170.[^cite_note-The-Times-of-Israel-20240612-367] It increased to 215 by the late afternoon.[^cite_note-BBC-News-20240612-368] On 13 June, Hezbollah fired 150 rockets and 30 UAVs at 15 targets in northern Israel and the Golan Heights.[^cite_note-369]

[Amos Hochstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amos_Hochstein), a senior advisor to US President [Joe Biden](145422.md), arrived in Israel on 17 June to attempt to de-escalate tensions between Israel and Lebanese militias.[^cite_note-370] On 19 June, Nasrallah stated that a Hezbollah invasion of the Galilee "remains on the table"[^cite_note-371][^cite_note-372] and alleged that the government of [Cyprus](5593.md) was allowing Israeli forces to operate within its country and thus made Cyprus a legitimate target for Hezbollah strikes. Cypriot president [Nikos Christodoulides](58652814.md) denied that Cyprus was taking sides in the conflict.[^cite_note-373] On 30 June, Israeli officials reported 18 IDF soldiers were wounded by a drone attack on the Golan Heights.[^cite_note-374] The inability of Israelis to return to settlements and homes in the north of the country led to [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) stating that Israel had effectively "lost sovereignty in the northern quadrant of its country".[^cite_note-375][^cite_note-376]

On 3 July, senior Hezbollah field commander Mohammed Nasser was killed in an Israeli airstrike in Tyre,[^cite_note-377] and in response Hezbollah fired 100 rockets at IDF positions.[^cite_note-378] The following day, it fired 200 more rockets into Israel.[^cite_note-379][^cite_note-380] On 9 July, an Israeli airstrike on the Beirut–Damascus highway in Syria killed two Hezbollah members,[^cite_note-381][^cite_note-382] and Hezbollah fired 40 rockets at the Golan Heights.[^cite_note-383] A week later, at least two people were wounded in an Israeli drone attack in Lebanon,[^cite_note-384] and Hezbollah rocket strikes in Kiryat Shmona damaged residential buildings.[^cite_note-385] On 22 July, drones from Lebanon were intercepted by Israeli forces,[^cite_note-Haaretz-2024-386] and a Hezbollah rocket strike injured two soldiers in northern Israel.[^cite_note-387] The following day and in response, Israel launched artillery strikes on southern Lebanon,[^cite_note-388] Hezbollah launched rocket strikes in northern Israel,[^cite_note-389] and Israeli warplanes and artillery struck southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-390]

### Further airstrikes (July – September 2024)

See also: [Majdal Shams attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majdal_Shams_attack) and [Killing of Fuad Shukr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Fuad_Shukr)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of the Israel–Hezbollah conflict (27 July 2024 – 16 September 2024)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Israel%E2%80%93Hezbollah_conflict_\(27_July_2024_%E2%80%93_16_September_2024\)).



The missile impact site on the soccer field of Majdal Shams, after the bodies of the deceased and wounded were removed

On 27 July, an Israeli strike in southern Lebanon killed four members of different militant groups.[^cite_note-Reuters-391] A [rocket strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majdal_Shams_attack) hit the [Druze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druze_in_Israel) town of [Majdal Shams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majdal_Shams) in the northern [Golan Heights](7515865.md), killing 12 children and injuring dozens more.[^cite_note-TOI27July-392][^cite_note-Ynetnews-2024-393] In retaliation, the IDF bombed villages and towns in south and east Lebanon the following day.[^cite_note-Gadzo-Varshalomidze-Pietromarchi-394][^cite_note-395] On 29 July, two were killed in an Israeli strike in Lebanon.[^cite_note-396] The next day, an [Israeli airstrike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Fuad_Shukr) struck Hezbollah infrastructure in southern Lebanon, and one Israeli civilian was killed in a rocket strike from Lebanon to northern Israel.[^cite_note-The-Jerusalem-Post-20240730a-397] Hezbollah said it fired at Israeli warplanes which entered Lebanese airspace.[^cite_note-398] The IDF said the airstrike targeted senior commander of Hezbollah [Fuad Shukr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuad_Shukr) for his involvement in the [Majdal Shams attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majdal_Shams_attack),[^cite_note-The-Jerusalem-Post-20240730b-399] and Hezbollah later confirmed his death.[^cite_note-400][^cite_note-401] Four civilians were also killed, while 80 people were injured.[^cite_note-402]

On 17 August, at least 11 including a woman and her two children were killed and four were wounded including two serious injuries in [an Israeli strike on a metal warehouse in Nabatieh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/August_2024_Nabatieh_attack).[^cite_note-403][^cite_note-Qiblawi-2024-404] The IDF claimed that it struck a Hezbollah "weapons storage facility".[^cite_note-Qiblawi-2024-404] The [Embassy of the Philippines, Beirut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_the_Philippines,_Beirut) advised its 11,000 citizens in Lebanon to leave the country following the strike.[^cite_note-405] A Hezbollah drone strike injured two soldiers in an IDF site near [Misgav Am](12960865.md).[^cite_note-406] One was killed in an Israeli drone strike on a motorcycle in Tyre. The IDF confirmed the death of a commander of the elite [Redwan Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redwan_Force).[^cite_note-407]

Israeli airstrikes on 25 August



[Israeli Defence Forces](39237.md) officials during the 25 August strikes

On 25 August, Israel began strikes in southern Lebanon against Hezbollah targets.[^cite_note-408][^cite_note-409][^cite_note-410] Israel framed its strikes as "preemptive".[^cite_note-CNN20240824-411][^cite_note-AxiosInfo-412][^cite_note-APNewsInfo-413] Hezbollah said that it fired over 320 Katyusha rockets to IDF sites in northern Israel and the Golan Heights as the first phase of response to the assassination of Fuad Shukr.[^cite_note-414][^cite_note-415][^cite_note-416] A woman was slightly injured due to [sharpnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharpnel) from the attack in [Acre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acre,_Israel).[^cite_note-417][^cite_note-418][^cite_note-419] Some homes were also damaged in Northern Israel.[^cite_note-420][^cite_note-421] Israel sent a hundred fighter jets during its operations.[^cite_note-422] Israeli Defense Minister [Yoav Gallant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Gallant) described the strikes as "very successful", stating that "over fifty percent, maybe two-thirds" of Hezbollah's strikes were prevented.[^cite_note-423] An Israeli drone strike in Qasimia in southern Lebanon wounded two people including one Hezbollah militant.[^cite_note-Adler-Uras-2024-424][^cite_note-425]

&#x20;Israel airstrikes struck Khiam, Zebqin, [Yater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yater), Shebaa, Nabatieh, Bir Kalb, Kfar Kila, [Aalma ech Chaab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aalma_ech_Chaab), and Mays al-Jabal, killing a Hezbollah militant and a [Amal Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amal_Movement) militant.[^cite_note-Adler-Uras-2024-424][^cite_note-426][^cite_note-427] Another three people were wounded, namely a Hezbollah militant and a Syrian national.[^cite_note-Adler-Uras-2024-424][^cite_note-428][^cite_note-429] An [Israeli Navy](1370788.md) officer was killed by a malfunctioning missile launched by the [Iron Dome](18342427.md) on a [Dvora-class fast patrol boat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dvora-class_fast_patrol_boat) off the coast of [Nahariya](323332.md), northern Israel and two others were injured.[^cite_note-IsraeliBoat-430][^cite_note-431][^cite_note-432] An Israel drone strike struck [Zawtar El Charkiyeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zawtar_El_Charkiyeh).[^cite_note-433][^cite_note-434] The Amal movement said one of its fighters from [Khiam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khiam) was killed. Hezbollah confirmed the death of six militants.[^cite_note-435][^cite_note-436] Departures and arrivals were canceled at [Ben Gurion Airport](113149.md) until 10 a.m.;[^cite_note-NYTimesInfo-437] two [El Al](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Al) flights were diverted.[^cite_note-APNewsInfo-413] The [Golan Regional Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golan_Regional_Council) instructed residents of the [Golan Heights](7515865.md) to remain in their shelters.[^cite_note-CNN20240824-411] Yoav Gallant declared an "emergency situation" for two days.[^cite_note-438][^cite_note-439] Representatives of the United States were monitoring the situation.[^cite_note-440][^cite_note-441]



[F-15I fighter](11715.md) used to attack targets in Lebanon on 27 September 2024

On 8 September, special forces from the [Shaldag Unit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaldag_Unit) [raided and destroyed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Masyaf_raid) an Iranian rocket manufacturing facility beneath a branch of the [Syrian Scientific Studies and Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Scientific_Studies_and_Research_Center) in [Masyaf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masyaf), which was used to supply Hezbollah with precision rockets.[^cite_note-442] On 16 September, the [Security Cabinet of Israel](22256400.md) approved a new war aim of returning evacuated residents to the north of Israel, adding to the goals of destroying Hamas and liberating the hostages.[^cite_note-443]

### Escalation (September – November 2024)

Main article: [2024 Lebanon war](78006796.md)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of the Israel–Hezbollah conflict (17 September – 26 November 2024)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Israel%E2%80%93Hezbollah_conflict_\(17_September_%E2%80%93_26_November_2024\)).

#### September attacks

See also: [2024 Lebanon electronic device attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Lebanon_electronic_device_attacks), [September 2024 Lebanon strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_2024_Lebanon_strikes), and [2024 Hezbollah headquarters strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Hezbollah_headquarters_strike)

On 17 September, at least 12 people were killed and more than 2,750 were wounded, including Hezbollah members and civilians, after [the explosions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Lebanon_electronic_device_attacks) of their [pagers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pager) in Lebanon and Syria, including in [Beirut](37428.md).[^cite_note-444] The attack resulted in 1,500 fighters being taken out of action, with many facing blindness or losing their hands.[^cite_note-445] Among those injured was the Iranian ambassador, [Mojtaba Amani](77897015.md).[^cite_note-446][^cite_note-447] Several drones launched from Lebanon crossed the border.[^cite_note-448] Hezbollah confirmed the death of 12 militants on that day.[^cite_note-449] Also on 17 September, the [Shin Bet](311752.md) claimed that it thwarted a Hezbollah attempt for assassinate a former senior defence official with a [Claymore mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claymore_mine).[^cite_note-450][^cite_note-451]

On 18 September [another series of explosions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Lebanon_electronic_device_attacks) involving wireless devices was reported across Lebanon, killing at least 30 people and injuring 750 others.[^cite_note-452][^cite_note-453][^cite_note-454] Hezbollah said that it carried out four strikes targeting IDF sites including in [Neve Ziv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neve_Ziv) and [Beit Hillel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Hillel) with rockets and artillery. Lebanese media reported that Israeli strikes struck Al-Jbeen, Shama, Majdal Zoun, Kfar Kila, Kfar Shiuba, Houla, al-Taybeh, the forest in the vicinity of Kounine and [Beit Yahoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Yahoun), and the outskirts of [Yater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yater).[^cite_note-455] The IDF said that it struck Hezbollah sites in Chihine, Tayibe, Blida, Meiss El Jabal, Aitaroun and Kfarkela and a Hezbollah weapons depot in [Khiam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khiam) in these airstrikes.[^cite_note-reuters20240919-456]

On 19 September, the IDF said that it started carrying out airstrikes against Hezbollah to destroy its capabilities in southern Lebanon while heavy bombing was reported in [Deir Qanoun En Nahr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deir_Qanoun_En_Nahr).[^cite_note-457] The IDF also said that it killed two Lebanese militants attempting to infiltrate the border and plant an explosive at an IDF post.[^cite_note-458] Two Israeli soldiers were killed and nine others were injured in Hezbollah attacks in the vicinity of the Lebanese border.[^cite_note-Siddiqui-Najjar-2024-459][^cite_note-460] Three rockets launched by Hezbollah to [Metula](2599433.md) caused fires and damaged electric infrastructure.[^cite_note-461] The IDF said that its jets struck more than a hundred Hezbollah rocket launchers and other Hezbollah sites in its heaviest air strikes since the Israel–Hezbollah conflict started in October 2023.[^cite_note-Al-Jazeera-20240919-462][^cite_note-reuters20240919-456] An Israeli strike in al-Haniyeh wounded four people.[^cite_note-463] The Lebanese National News Agency reported that Israeli airstrikes targeted Mahmoudieh, Ksar al-Aroush, and Birket Jabbour.[^cite_note-Al-Jazeera-20240919-462]

On 20 September, an Israeli [strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/20_September_2024_Beirut_attack) in the [Dahieh](6082788.md) suburb of Beirut killed [Ibrahim Aqil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibrahim_Aqil), the acting commander of the elite [Redwan Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redwan_Force) and other personnel including Ahmad Mahmoud Wahabi, a senior commander of Hezbollah while they were in a meeting. At least 45 people were killed including Aqil, Wahabi, another 14 high-ranking Hezbollah militants, three children, and seven women, while 68 people were injured. Heavy rocket fire was reported afterward in northern Israel. Hezbollah claimed that its Katyusha rockets struck the IDF base in Meron. Hezbollah also said that it targeted Israeli airbases, intelligence bases, and a tank.[^cite_note-464][^cite_note-465][^cite_note-466]

On 21 September, the IDF claimed to have "almost completely dismantled" Hezbollah's military chain of command.[^cite_note-467] The IDF claimed that it destroyed thousands of rocket launchers.[^cite_note-468] Four people were wounded in Israeli strikes.[^cite_note-469] The IAF also launched heavy artillery strikes in several areas in southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-470] The IDF said that it launched 400 strikes targeting Hezbollah rocket launchers and other infrastructure.[^cite_note-471]

On 22 September, Israeli President [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md) denied any Israeli involvement in the pager explosions.[^cite_note-independent20240922-472] Hezbollah made two attacks on the Israeli [Ramat David Airbase](5340731.md) using Fadi 1 and Wadi 2 missiles, injuring one person;[^cite_note-473][^cite_note-474] an additional three people were also injured in [Krayot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krayot) and [Lower Galilee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lower_Galilee).[^cite_note-475] Three people were killed in Israeli strikes in southern Lebanon. Hezbollah confirmed the death of two of its militants.[^cite_note-476] Hezbollah claimed that it struck three Israeli targets.[^cite_note-477]

Hezbollah rockets explode near a highway in Israel on 24 September

On 23 September, the IDF conducted over [1,600 strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_2024_Israeli_attacks_against_Lebanon) in Lebanon, killing at least 558 people and injuring more than 1,835 others including children, women and paramedics according to the Lebanese Health Ministry.[^cite_note-aljazeera20240924-60][^cite_note-478][^cite_note-479] Hezbollah fired about 240[^cite_note-Washington-Post-2024-480] rockets into Israel, the West Bank, and Golan Heights, injuring five people. It first fired 35 rockets into northern Israel targeting IDF bases and warehouses, lightly injuring a man in the Lower Galilee.[^cite_note-481] It later fired around 80 rockets, targeting several locations including [Ariel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariel_\(Israeli_settlement\)) and [Karnei Shomron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karnei_Shomron) in the occupied West Bank and [Haifa](55606.md).[^cite_note-482][^cite_note-483] The group targeted IDF bases and [Rafael Advanced Defense Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Advanced_Defense_Systems) facilities.[^cite_note-484] [Ali Karaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Karaki), the commander of Hezbollah's Southern Front, was targeted in an Israeli airstrike in Dahieh.[^cite_note-485][^cite_note-486] Hezbollah said that he survived the attack.[^cite_note-487] A Hamas field commander was killed in an Israeli airstrike in southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-488]

&#x20;By 25 September, an army of around 40,000 Iraqi, Syrian, and Houthi militants were concentrated around the Golan Heights, which the [Institute for the Study of War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_the_Study_of_War) argued was to defend against an Israeli invasion of Lebanon.[^cite_note-489] The number of displaced Lebanese people reached an estimated 500,000.[^cite_note-490][^cite_note-491] On 26 September, an Israeli strike on a three-story building in [Younine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Younine) killed at least 19 Syrians and a Lebanese person, mostly women or children, and injured eight others.[^cite_note-492][^cite_note-493]
