[Yoav Galant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Galant), [Herzi Halevi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herzi_Halevi) and [Tomer Bar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomer_Bar) on 28 September 2024

On 27 September, Israel [struck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Hezbollah_headquarters_strike) Hezbollah's central headquarters in Beirut, targeting Hezbollah leader [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md).[^cite_note-494] Al-Manar reported that four buildings collapsed in the attack. At least five other people died, including Ali Karaki, Hezbollah's commander of the southern front since 1982, and at least 100 were injured.[^cite_note-apnews20240928-92][^cite_note-495][^cite_note-496][^cite_note-497]

On 28 September, the [Lebanese National News Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_News_Agency_\(Lebanon\)) reported that IDF strikes struck civil defense centres and a medical clinic in [Taybeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taybeh_\(Marjaayoun\)) and [Deir Siriane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deir_Siriane) killing 11 medical staff and injuring 10 others.[^cite_note-498] On 29 September, an Israeli air strike on a home in [Dahr-al-Ain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dahr-al-Ain) killed at least 11 people.[^cite_note-499][^cite_note-500] The Lebanese National News Agency reported that at least 17 members of a family were killed and several others were trapped under rubble in an Israeli air strike in Zboud.[^cite_note-501] An Israeli strike in [Ain El Delb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ain_El_Delb) killed 45 people and injured at least 75 others.[^cite_note-aljazeera20240930-502][^cite_note-503] An Israeli strike in Bekaa killed 12 people and injured 20 others.[^cite_note-aljazeera20240930-502]

On 30 September, Israel informed the [United States](3434750.md) that it intended to carry out a ground maneuver in [Lebanon](17771.md) aimed at clearing [Hezbollah](13919.md)'s infrastructure along the border. That evening, the [Lebanese Armed Forces](17779.md) (LAF) and UNIFIL withdrew from the [Israeli-Lebanese border](987817.md) to the north to a distance of 5 kilometres (3.1 mi; 2.7 nmi) from the border, while the IDF designated the settlements of Metula, [Misgav Am](12960865.md) and [Kfar Giladi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Giladi) as closed military areas.[^cite_note-504] Israeli troops amassed on the border in [southern Lebanon](4205968.md), with Israel stating that they were preparing for a limited ground invasion.[^cite_note-505]

#### Israeli invasion of Lebanon

Main article: [2024 Lebanon war](78006796.md)

On 1 October, the Israeli military began an [invasion of southern Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israeli_invasion_of_Lebanon),[^cite_note-506][^cite_note-507][^cite_note-508] also known as the Third Lebanon War by some Israeli sources,[^cite_note-Third-War-93] and announced that Israel had been carrying out small covert raids into Lebanon for months.[^cite_note-509] The IDF announced that it had launched "limited, localized and targeted raids" against targets with strategic importance to Hezbollah.[^cite_note-510] An Israeli strike on a house in Al-Dawoudiya killed at least 10 people and injured five others.[^cite_note-511] Hours after the initial Israeli raids began, Iran [launched ballistic missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_2024_Iranian_strikes_on_Israel) against Israel,[^cite_note-512] citing "terrorist acts of the Zionist regime" such as the [assassination of Hassan Nasrallah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Hassan_Nasrallah) as justification for the attack.[^cite_note-513]

On 3 October, six days after Nasrallah's assassination, his successor, [Hashem Safieddine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hashem_Safieddine), was also assassinated during [an Israeli airstrike in Beirut.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Hashem_Safieddine)[^cite_note-514] Israel kept the extent of its campaign vague.[^cite_note-515] On 29 October, IDF officers said that while some Lebanese villages still pose a threat to Israel, most military goals in Lebanon border area were fulfilled and conditions were created to cement Israel's achievements in ceasefire negotiations.[^cite_note-516]

On 26 November, hours after IDF troops reached the [Litani River](1920925.md),[^cite_note-517] [Prime Minister of Israel](24898.md) [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) announced the transfer of a [sixty-day ceasefire deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israel%E2%80%93Lebanon_ceasefire_agreement) to the [Security Cabinet of Israel](22256400.md). Support for the deal in the [Israeli government](5006115.md) was "unanimous".[^cite_note-Quillen20241126-518] The deal involved Hezbollah operatives withdrawing to the north of the Litani River, and Israel withdrawing from Lebanon at the end of the sixty days. The deal also stated that Israel would retain "complete military freedom of action" to attack Lebanon in the event of a violation of the agreement by Hezbollah or another entity in Lebanon.[^cite_note-Quillen20241126-518][^cite_note-519][^cite_note-520] [Prime Minister of Lebanon](508949.md) [Najib Mikati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Najib_Mikati) issued strong support for the agreement and urged the international community to help implement the agreement immediately in order to "halt Israeli aggression".[^cite_note-Quillen20241126-518] On 27 November, thousands of displaced Lebanese began to return to areas ruined by Israeli strikes.[^cite_note-upi20241127-95]

### Ceasefire violations (November 2024 – March 2026)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of the Israel–Hezbollah conflict (27 November 2024 – 26 February 2026)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Israel%E2%80%93Hezbollah_conflict_\(27_November_2024_%E2%80%93_26_February_2026\)).

On 3 December, Hezbollah fired two projectiles at the [Shebaa Farms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shebaa_Farms) in what it described as a "defensive and warning response", accusing Israel of committing repeated ceasefire violations. In response, the IDF conducted a wave of strikes in southern Lebanon, killing nine people. Two people, including a Lebanese security services corporal, were killed by Israeli strikes earlier that day.[^cite_note-521] On 9 December, four reservists of the [35th Paratroopers Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/35th_Paratroopers_Brigade_\(Israel\)) were killed in an accident when unmarked munitions exploded in a tunnel in the [Labbouneh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labbouneh?action=edit\&redlink=1) area, which detonated Hezbollah weaponry, leading to the tunnel's collapse.[^cite_note-522] The IDF withdrew from [Al-Khiyam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Khiyam) on 12 December in accordance with the ceasefire.[^cite_note-523] On 26 January 2025, a day before the ceasefire's deadline, Israeli forces fired at Lebanese residents en route to communities near the border, killing 22 people and injuring 124.[^cite_note-524] After the ceasefire's initial deadline passed on 27 January, Israeli troops refused to withdraw from southern Lebanon, accusing the Lebanese government of not upholding the deal. The ceasefire was extended to 18 February.[^cite_note-525]

On 13 February, the IDF accused Iran's [Quds Force](4028355.md) of smuggling cash onto civilian flights en route to [Beirut–Rafic Hariri International Airport](12869180.md) to fund Hezbollah.[^cite_note-526] In response, Lebanon blocked an Iranian passenger plane from entering its territory. The decision sparked protests from Hezbollah supporters, who blocked access to the airport, and on 15 February, attacked a [UNIFIL](787766.md) convoy en route to the airport, injuring deputy force commander [Chok Bahadur Dhakal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chok_Bahadur_Dhakal?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-527][^cite_note-528] On 17 February, an Israeli drone strike hit a car in Sidon, killing Mohammed Shaheen, Hamas's operations chief in Lebanon.[^cite_note-529] On the ceasefire's deadline on 18 February, the IDF withdrew from all of southern Lebanon, apart from five hilltops on the border—namely al-Aziyah, al-Awaida, el-Hamames, Jabal Bilat, and Labbouneh.[^cite_note-apnews20250218-530][^cite_note-531]

On 22 March, six rockets were fired at [Metula](2599433.md) from southern Lebanon, with three crashing in Lebanon and the rest being intercepted. Hezbollah denied responsibility for the attack. The IDF responded with strikes on dozens of Hezbollah targets across southern Lebanon, including rocket launchers.[^cite_note-532] On 25 March, an Israeli drone strike on a vehicle in [Qaaqaait al-Jisr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qaaqaait_al-Jisr) killed Hassan Kamal Halawi, who commanded Hezbollah's anti-tank missile unit in southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-533] On 28 March, two rockets were fired at [Kiryat Shmona](323369.md), with one falling short in Lebanon and the other being intercepted.[^cite_note-534] In response, the IDF said that it struck several Hezbollah targets in southern Lebanon, including command centers. A strike in [Kfar Tebnit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Tebnit) killed three people and injured 18 others. An airstrike also hit a building in [Dahieh](6082788.md) that the IDF accused of storing drones from Hezbollah's Unit 127, in the first attack in [Beirut](37428.md) since the ceasefire went into effect.[^cite_note-535]

On 1 April, an Israeli airstrike on an apartment in Dahieh killed four people and injured seven others. Among the dead were senior Hezbollah official Hassan Bdeir, who the IDF accused of planning to attack Israeli civilians alongside Hamas, and his son, who was a Hezbollah member.[^cite_note-536] On 5 June, the IDF said that it struck underground facilities in Dahieh that were used by Hezbollah's Unit 127 to produce thousands of drones with funding from Iran. The strikes marked the largest escalation of the conflict since the ceasefire.[^cite_note-537][^cite_note-538] On 24 October, two people were killed and two others were injured in an [Israeli](9282173.md) [airstrike](323167.md) on a vehicle in [Toul, Lebanon](78327469.md).[^cite_note-539] The IDF said that it struck the head of logistics in Hezbollah's Southern Front Abbas Hassan Karaki whose presence in the south of Lebanon violated the ceasefire terms.[^cite_note-540]

On 18 November, [an Israeli strike](81633803.md) hit a parked car at the [Ain al-Hilweh](5901773.md) Palestinian refugee camp, killing 13 people. The IDF said it hit a Hamas training camp.[^cite_note-541] On 23 November, [an Israeli strike](81670215.md) in [Haret Hreik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haret_Hreik) killed five people and injured 28 others. [Haytham Ali Tabatabai](81670215.md), Hezbollah's chief of staff and second-in-command, was killed in the attack.[^cite_note-542] On the one-year anniversary of the ceasefire, [The New York Times](30680.md) characterized the conflict as still ongoing with "near-daily" attacks by Israel into Lebanon.[^cite_note-543] On 20 February 2026, Israeli airstrikes in the [Baalbek](58207.md) area killed ten people, including eight Hezbollah members, and injured 24 others. Three of the dead were identified as local Hezbollah commanders.[^cite_note-544] A separate strike that day in [Ain al-Hilweh](5901773.md) killed two people, which the IDF said hit a Hamas command center.[^cite_note-545] On 21 February, Arab media reported that IRGC officers took responsibility for supervising Hezbollah's activities, increasing its military preparedness in anticipation of an Israeli or American attack amid the [Iran–United States crisis](82533582.md).[^cite_note-546]

### 2026 Lebanon war (March 2026 – present)

Main article: [2026 Lebanon war](82549840.md)

Further information: [2026 Iran war](82533582.md)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of the 2026 Lebanon war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_2026_Lebanon_war).

On 2 March, Hezbollah fired several rockets at northern Israel, setting off sirens in Haifa and the Upper Galilee.[^cite_note-547] The group said its actions were in response to [the assassination](82537558.md) of Iranian supreme leader [Ali Khamenei](385653.md). One of the rockets was intercepted, while several others hit open areas.[^cite_note-548][^cite_note-549] Hezbollah later claimed that the attack was a "defensive act" after over a year of Israeli attacks despite a [truce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israel%E2%80%93Lebanon_ceasefire_agreement).[^cite_note-550] It added that it restarted fighting to force Israel to stop its aggression and evacuate from [seized Lebanese territories](6020082.md), emphasising that the move was unrelated to the [Iran war](82533582.md).[^cite_note-551] The IAF and Israeli Navy conducted extensive retaliatory strikes across Lebanon, mainly targeting Dahieh and southern Lebanon. The IDF said it struck over 70 targets, while issuing evacuation orders for over 50 villages in the south and east. It added that Hussein Meklad, Hezbollah's intelligence chief, was killed.[^cite_note-552] At least 52 people were killed, 154 were injured, and 29,000 others were displaced, according to the Lebanese Health Ministry.[^cite_note-553] In response to Hezbollah's actions, prime minister [Nawaf Salam](21077090.md) banned the group from conducting military activities.[^cite_note-554]

On 3 March, Israel resumed ground operations in Lebanon and continued conducting strikes. Attacks hit weapons facilities, command centers, and communication equipment, along with the studios of Hezbollah-linked outlets [Al-Nour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Nour) and [Al-Manar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Manar). The IDF said it struck 160 targets since the escalation and announced that it killed a senior liaison to the IRGC in Beirut the day prior.[^cite_note-555] It separately killed Davoud Alizadeh, the commander of the [Quds Force](4028355.md)'s Lebanon Corps, in [Tehran](57654.md).[^cite_note-556] In the night, Hezbollah fired rockets at Tel Aviv and Haifa.[^cite_note-557] By 4 March, more than 300,000 southern Lebanese residents had been displaced due to the conflict.[^cite_note-558] Israeli strikes hit residential areas in Beirut and eastern Lebanon, killing at least 11 people.[^cite_note-559] Two Israeli soldiers were wounded in Lebanon after Hezbollah claimed to have struck a [Merkava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merkava) tank in [Houla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houla,_Lebanon).[^cite_note-560] On 6 March, a Hezbollah rocket strike in northern Israel injured eight soldiers, five seriously.[^cite_note-561] Later that night, Israeli commandos raided [Al-Nabi Shayth](6016255.md) in an attempt to recover the remains of missing pilot [Ron Arad](8015452.md), leading to a gunbattle that left three Lebanese soldiers dead. Simultaneous airstrikes in the area killed 41 people and injured 40 more. The Israelis withdrew without finding Arad's body.[^cite_note-562][^cite_note-563]

On 8 March, two Israeli soldiers were killed in a separate rocket attack in southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-564] The following day, Hezbollah missiles struck a satellite communications center near [Beit Shemesh](323292.md) and a daycare in [Ramla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramla) in central Israel.[^cite_note-565] On 1 April, an Israeli naval strike in Beirut killed Haj Youssef ⁠Ismail Hashem, the commander of Hezbollah's Southern Front.[^cite_note-566]

On 8 April, Israeli strikes killed 254 people and wounded more than 1,000 others in central Beirut. [Israel Katz](19041378.md) said that the strikes targeted hundreds of Hezbollah militants and command centers across Lebanon, in the group's largest blow since the [pager attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Lebanon_electronic_device_attacks).[^cite_note-567] On 16 April, President Trump announced that Israel and Lebanon agreed to a 10-day [truce](82970933.md).[^cite_note-aljazeera20260416-96] On 27 April, Hezbollah leader [Naim Qassem](28105310.md) stated that the group will not revert to the pre-March status and will respond to Israeli attacks.[^cite_note-568] On 15 May, the truce was extended by Israel and Lebanon for another 45 days.[^cite_note-reuters20260515-98] On 1 June, Israel agreed not to hit Beirut's southern suburbs, and Hezbollah agreed not to attack Israel as part of a US proposal, with the cease-fire framework to be expanded to include entire Lebanon.[^cite_note-thehindu20260601-99] On 3 June, Israel and Lebanon agreed to renew the ceasefire, mediated by the US, and plan to establish "pilot zones".[^cite_note-firstpost20260604-100] On 4 June, Hezbollah rejected the truce deal announced the previous day and instead demanded a comprehensive truce and full withdrawal of Israel from Lebanon.[^cite_note-aawsat20260604-101]

[Israeli forces](39237.md) carried out airstrikes on the [Dahieh](6082788.md) area in Beirut's southern suburbs on 14 June 2026, targeting what they described as Hezbollah infrastructure in the Dahieh area. The office of the Israeli prime minister said the strikes were in response to rocket fire from Hezbollah toward northern Israel earlier the same day. [Lebanese civil defence](37101752.md) reported at least three people were killed in the strikes.[^cite_note-569][^cite_note-570]

According to [Axios](52436164.md), U.S. officials said Israel had notified the Trump administration before the operation.[^cite_note-571]

On 19 June, President Trump said that Israel and Hezbollah agreed to a truce mediated by the US, Qatar and Iran.[^cite_note-572][^cite_note-573]

On 26 June, US Secretary of State [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) announced a framework deal between Israel and Lebanon that aims to achieve "lasting peace and security" through US mediation. The agreement asks for a cease-fire, with Hezbollah agreeing to terminate all hostilities and withdraw from southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-574] It was later rejected by Hezbollah.[^cite_note-575]

## Impact and aftermath

[Syrian rebels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_opposition_\(2011%E2%80%932024\)) took advantage of the war, the heavy losses suffered by Hezbollah, and Hezbollah's absence in Syria to launch the [2024 Syrian opposition offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Syrian_opposition_offensive) on 27 November 2024 to overrun Syria's government-held territories.[^cite_note-576] Hezbollah, which had previously sent forces, said they would not deploy troops, but later sent "supervising forces" to unsuccessfully attempt to repel the [2024 Homs offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Homs_offensive), as Homs is a key transport route for Iran to supply Hezbollah.[^cite_note-577] In December 2024, [the fall of Assad's Baathist regime](78548066.md) in Syria was another blow to its Lebanese ally, Hezbollah, which was already weakened because of Israeli military actions.[^cite_note-578]

Following major operations in 2024, numerous analysts and experts have characterized the outcome as a significant victory for Israel. The Institute for the Study of War reported that Israeli operations effectively degraded Hezbollah's capabilities, leading to a ceasefire agreement on November 26, 2024, which mandated [Hezbollah's disarmament](80353105.md) in southern Lebanon. Hezbollah failed to accomplish its goal of compelling Israel to cease operations in the Gaza Strip, resulting in a weakened position within the region. Similarly, experts from the Atlantic Council noted that Israel's military actions, including the elimination of Hezbollah's leader, Hassan Nasrallah, significantly diminished the group's operational effectiveness.[^cite_note-israels-victory-lebanon-3][^cite_note-579]

## Casualties and damage

### Lebanon

|  | This section needs to be **updated**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(November 2024)* |
| --- | --- |







**Top, left to right:** Destruction in [Houla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houla,_Lebanon) and [Maroun al-Ras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maroun_al-Ras) pictured on 15 December 2024\
**Bottom:** Aftermath of the [October 2024 Bachoura airstrike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_2024_Bachoura_airstrike)

The olive trade in southern Lebanon, which is the main source of income for many, was halted as farmers stopped their harvests in fear of the active shelling.[^cite_note-580][^cite_note-581] According to Agriculture Minister [Abbas Hajj Hassan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abbas_Hajj_Hassan?action=edit\&redlink=1), 40,000 olive trees have been burned down by fires caused by IDF shelling.[^cite_note-582] The ministry has also said that 790 hectares of agricultural land have been damaged and 340 thousand farm animals have been lost.[^cite_note-reliefwebint-2024a-583] In total, the IDF has caused US\$3 billion of agriculture losses.[^cite_note-584] The [Institute of International Finance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_of_International_Finance) predicted that Lebanon's GDP could decline by one percent by the end of 2023 and by 30 percent in 2024 in the event of further spillover of the war.[^cite_note-585] According to the Council for the South, Israeli strikes have destroyed 1,700 buildings and damaged 14,000 others and caused US\$500 million worth of damage on water, electricity, telecoms and other infrastructure.[^cite_note-three-killed-in-south-lebanon-strikes-49]

#### Human losses and displacement

See also: [Lebanese displacement during the Israel–Hezbollah conflict (2023–present)](77973245.md)

| Phase | Killed | Injured | Additional notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Early phase and [invasion](78006796.md): 8 October 2023 – 26 November 2024[^cite_note-586][^cite_note-587] | 3,823 | 15,859 | At least 80% killed after the [escalation in September](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_2024_Israeli_attacks_against_Lebanon)[^cite_note-588] |
| [Ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israel%E2%80%93Lebanon_ceasefire_agreement): 27 November 2024 – 1 March 2026[^cite_note-589] | 397[^cite_note-590] | 1,102 |  |
| [2026 Lebanon war](82549840.md) and [ceasefire](82970933.md): 2 March 2026 – present[^cite_note-Lebanon-66] | 3,980 | 12,001 |  |
| Total | 8,200 | 28,962 |  |

On 4 December 2024, the [Lebanese Health Ministry](77943319.md) reported that since 7 October 2023, Israeli attacks killed 4,047 people, including 316 children and 790 women, and injured 16,638 others.[^cite_note-591] Among the dead were at least 41 Lebanese Army soldiers[^cite_note-592] and more than 200 medics.[^cite_note-593] Several UNIFIL workers and peacekeepers were injured in numerous attacks by both Israel and Lebanese militias.[^cite_note-594] By 25 November 2025, the Lebanese Health Ministry reported that Israeli attacks during the ceasefire had killed 331 people and injured 945. The UN said that at least 127 fatalities were civilians.[^cite_note-595] At the height of the conflict in October 2024, more than 1.2 million Lebanese were displaced, including between 200,000 and 300,000 who fled into Syria.[^cite_note-596]

#### Infrastructure

The conflict resulted in widespread destruction in Lebanon that was particularly seen in the south, east, and [Dahieh](6082788.md)—the southern suburbs of the capital, Beirut. According to the World Bank, 99,209 houses were damaged during the conflict, of which 18% were destroyed.[^cite_note-apnews20250112-597] By 31 October 2024, nearly 25% of the buildings in towns near the border were damaged or destroyed, with the most destruction occurring in [Kfar Kila](7299326.md) and [Ayta ash-Shaab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayta_ash-Shaab). Roughly 80% of the damage occurred after 2 October.[^cite_note-598] In the areas that the IDF had captured during its ground invasion into Lebanon, 42% of buildings were destroyed, according to [NBC News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBC_News).[^cite_note-599]

[Amnesty International](18947898.md) reported the damaging or destruction of over 10,000 buildings between October 2024 and January 2025, with 70% of the buildings in [Yaroun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yaroun), [Dhayra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhayra?action=edit\&redlink=1), and [Al-Bustan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Bustan,_Lebanon) being severely damaged or destroyed.[^cite_note-600] [UN-Habitat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN-Habitat) reported that 27% of buildings in [Marjayoun](6236310.md) and 15% of buildings in [Bint Jbeil](6124393.md) were damaged or destroyed,[^cite_note-hrw20250217-601] while the mayor of Kfar Kila stated that 90% of the homes in the village were destroyed during the conflict, with the remaining 10% sustaining damage.[^cite_note-apnews20250218-530] The scale of destruction in Dahieh was comparable to the [2006 Lebanon war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Lebanon_War), with 353 buildings in the area being destroyed and more than 6,000 houses damaged. The Hezbollah-linked newspaper [Al Akhbar](7614001.md) reported that the cost of damages in Dahieh was \$630 million.[^cite_note-602][^cite_note-apnews20250112-597]

More than 60 schools have been destroyed since September 2024, according to [Save the Children](436885.md). The Lebanese Health Ministry said that Israeli strikes damaged 68 hospitals, 63 primary healthcare facilities, and 177 ambulances. The [World Health Organization](33583.md) reported that 47% of the attacks on health facilities resulted in fatalities. According to the [United Nations Development Programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Development_Programme), Israeli attacks in the districts of [Nabatieh](43794798.md), Bint Jbiel, [Tyre](30796.md), [Baalbek](58207.md), and [Baabda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baabda) damaged 48% of businesses, 36 health facilities, 40 water facilities, 18 telecommunication facilities, 36 public electricity facilities, and 83 educational institutions.[^cite_note-hrw20250217-601]

#### Economy

According to the [World Bank](36752.md)'s Lebanon Rapid Damage and Needs Assessment (RDNA) 2025 report, the conflict cost Lebanon a total of US\$14 billion. Physical damages amounted to \$6.8 billion, while decreased productivity, lost revenue, and operational costs amounted to \$7.2 billion. With \$4.6 billion in damages, the housing sector suffered the most losses, followed by the commerce, industry, and tourism sector with losses of \$3.4 billion. The conflict caused Lebanon's [real GDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_gross_domestic_product) to decline by 7.1% in 2024. The World Bank added that reconstruction costs amounted to \$11 billion.[^cite_note-603][^cite_note-604]

### Hezbollah

Since 8 October 2023, Hezbollah officially announced the names of 521 members killed during the conflict. After [Israel's escalation against Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_2024_Israeli_attacks_against_Lebanon) in September 2024, Hezbollah mostly stopped announcing its casualties.[^cite_note-605] As of December 2024, the IDF estimates that around 3,800 Hezbollah fighters had been killed since 8 October 2023, including 2,672 during the invasion of Lebanon and 44 since the ceasefire.[^cite_note-Fabian-20241225-606] It earlier estimated that at least 7,000 suffered injuries that made them unable to fight.[^cite_note-Fabian-20241128-607] [The Institute for National Security Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Institute_for_National_Security_Studies) of [Tel Aviv University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tel_Aviv_University) put losses at around 2,450.[^cite_note-608] According to a source familiar with Hezbollah's operations, the group possibly lost up to 4,000 members during the conflict—over 10 times its losses during the [2006 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Lebanon_War).[^cite_note-609][^cite_note-ReutersHezbollahRecovery-71]



Coffins of senior Hezbollah official [Hashem Safieddine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hashem_Safieddine) and secretary-general [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md) during [their funeral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Funeral_of_Hassan_Nasrallah) on 23 February 2025.

Hezbollah's senior leadership faced the heaviest casualties in its entire history during the conflict. According to the IDF, 13 members of Hezbollah's senior command were killed.[^cite_note-Fabian-20241225-606] Its political council lost figures such as [Secretary-General](9364927.md) [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md), who ruled the group for over three decades, and his deputy, [Hashem Safieddine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hashem_Safieddine). Its armed wing lost key leaders such as [Fuad Shukr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuad_Shukr), Hezbollah's military commander; [Ibrahim Aqil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibrahim_Aqil), Shukr's deputy; and [Ali Karaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Karaki), the commander of Hezbollah's Southern Front.[^cite_note-610][^cite_note-iranintl20241127-611] Hezbollah also saw heavy damage inflicted upon its equipment, missile arsenal and infrastructure in southern Lebanon, weakening its operational capacity. Heavy damage was inflicted upon military infrastructure such as bases, [tunnels](78024047.md), weapon depots, and fortifications, and its arms supply has been disrupted by Israeli strikes on Syrian border crossings.[^cite_note-Economist2024-94][^cite_note-iranintl20241127-611][^cite_note-612] According to IDF, they confiscated over 155,000 weapons and pieces of military equipment belonging to Hezbollah, including around 12,000 explosive devices, drones, and other explosive weapons, more than 13,000 anti-tank missile launchers and rockets, and anti-aircraft missiles. In addition, over 121,000 communication devices, computers, electronic equipment and documents were also captured.[^cite_note-613]

In April 2025, in response to increasing calls for its disarmament, Hezbollah expressed willingness to engage in discussions about its weapons with Lebanese President Joseph Aoun. These discussions would be contingent upon Israel's withdrawal from five hilltop positions in southern Lebanon and the cessation of its military strikes. President Aoun, who assumed office in January 2025, aims to consolidate all arms under state control and plans to initiate talks with Hezbollah amid growing domestic and international pressure. The 2024 conflict with Israel significantly weakened Hezbollah, resulting in the loss of key leaders and a substantial portion of its arsenal. A U.S.-brokered ceasefire requires the Lebanese army to dismantle unauthorized military facilities, particularly south of the Litani River. While Hezbollah may consider transferring its heaviest weapons to the army, it insists that Israel must first meet its demands. Discussions are underway among various Lebanese political leaders and institutions, including Parliament Speaker Nabih Berri and the Maronite Church. The U.S. has reiterated its call for Hezbollah's disarmament, and several Lebanese ministers advocate for a disarmament timetable based on post-civil war precedents. Hezbollah maintains it no longer has an armed presence south of the Litani, though Israel disputes this, accusing the group of retaining military infrastructure. Hezbollah emphasized diplomacy but warned of other options if Israel does not comply.[^cite_note-614][^cite_note-615]

In July 2025, it was reported that Hezbollah had lost 10,000 fighters during the war, and that its military capabilities had also deteriorated significantly.[^cite_note-616]

Israel's efforts against Hezbollah have focused on its military assets as well as its finances, with an emphasis on the estimated \$700 million directed from Iran each year through intermediaries that accounted for most of Hezbollah's annual operating budget. Some of the earliest Israeli military efforts in March 2026 targeted the organization's supply of gold and foreign currency, as well as branches of the [Al-Qard Al-Hasan Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qard_Al-Hasan_Association), a non-profit financial institution that has been described as being "Hezbollah's bank in Lebanon". Money changing offices and gas stations were also struck, all as part of an effort to squeeze the organization's sources of funding. With the overland route through Syria cutoff in the wake of the [Fall of the Assad regime](78548066.md) in December 2024, Iranian funding efforts shifted to [Beirut–Rafic Hariri International Airport](12869180.md), where Hezbollah influence allowed arms and cash to flow through. Under pressure from the United States, the Lebanese government blocked flights from Iran and took over security control of the airport. The [Banque du Liban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banque_du_Liban) implemented controls that limited the ability of Hezbollah to accept international money transfers; restrictions were placed on connections between Lebanon's formal banking system and Al-Qard al-Hassan. To bypass these constraints, the [hawala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawala) system had been used to send \$1 billion in Iranian funds in 2025 via a network of broker transfers that passed through [Iraq](7515928.md), [Turkey](11125639.md) and the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md), though sanctions have been placed on these brokers by the [United States Department of the Treasury](53667.md).[^cite_note-617]

### Israel









**Clockwise from top:** Damage to buildings in [Kiryat Shmona](323369.md) and homes in [Katzrin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katzrin) and forest fires due to rocket attacks from Lebanon

Forty-six Israeli civilians,[^cite_note-618] 28 soldiers and one security officer were killed in Hezbollah's attacks on Israel. An additional three soldiers were killed in non-combat incidents; one by malfunctioning ammunition and the other two in a tank accident. Forty-seven soldiers, including a civilian researcher posthumously recognized as a soldier, were killed during combat in southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-shinbet1-75][^cite_note-619] Hezbollah's attacks resulted in the evacuation of over 90,000 people—60,000 forced and 30,000 voluntary—from northern Israel.[^cite_note-Rosenberg-2024-620] As of July 2024, the Israeli government issued orders for the evacuation of 43 settlements located within 3 miles (4.8 km) of the border with Lebanon.[^cite_note-621] At least eight Israeli UAVs were shot down over Lebanon: four [Hermes 450](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hermes_450) and four [Hermes 900](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hermes_900) models.[^cite_note-622][^cite_note-623][^cite_note-624]

According to the [Israeli Army Radio](5242192.md), 2,874 buildings have been reported damaged by Hezbollah, including 841 in need of rebuilding. Property damage was estimated to be around [₪](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_new_shekel)1 billion (US\$273 million). As of November 2024, over 60% of the buildings in [Metula](2599433.md) were destroyed by Hezbollah attacks since the start of the conflict.[^cite_note-625] Three-fourths of the buildings in [Manara](3577562.md),[^cite_note-626] 382 buildings in [Kiryat Shmona](323369.md), and 55,000 acres of nature in northern Israel and the Golan Heights have been damaged or destroyed by Hezbollah's attacks, while major damage also took place in [Nahariya](323332.md) and [Shlomi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shlomi,_Israel).[^cite_note-627]

#### Economy

According to [Tel Aviv University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tel_Aviv_University) economist Tomer Fadlon, there would be long-term and significant effects on northern Israel's economy if its population did not return. He further said that even if Israel achieved victory in Gaza, the north would possibly not see an economic recovery.[^cite_note-Sullivan-2024-628] The conflict severely hurt the [tourism industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Israel) in the north, which employs many people. Many factories in the region have not shut down but suffered labor shortages of up to 50%.[^cite_note-Rosenberg-2024-620] According to an estimate by the [Bank of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_Israel) in November 2023, the [Israeli economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_economy) suffers the loss of 590 million [shekels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_new_shekel) (\$158 million) every week due absence of work caused by the evacuation of 144,000 people after the Gaza war, 40% of them from the north.[^cite_note-Sullivan-2024-628]

Once-robust communities such as [Snir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snir) and [Kiryat Shmona](323369.md) were mostly emptied following the evacuation orders in northern Israel. Kiryat Shmona was planned to be used by [food technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_technology) businesses, but companies left following the city's evacuation in October 2023. Northern Israel has one-third of Israel's agricultural land, and the occupied [Golan Heights](7515865.md) and [Galilee](12639.md) accounted for 73% of Israel's annual [egg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egg) production; however, after the outbreak of the conflict, few people remained in the north to tend chicken coops, resulting in a decline in domestic egg production.[^cite_note-629] In August 2024, the [Ministry of Agriculture and Food Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Agriculture_and_Food_Security_\(Israel\)) approved a quota for the import of 45 million eggs due to the anticipated drop in local production over the conflict and other reasons.[^cite_note-630]

## War crimes

### Israeli war crimes

See also: [Israeli war crimes in the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_war_crimes_in_the_Gaza_war) and [Gaza genocide](75668427.md)

#### Killing of Lebanese civilians

In September 2024, thousands of [wireless communication devices exploded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Lebanon_pager_explosions) throughout Lebanon and Syria in an attack attributed to Israel, killing dozens of people, including civilians and Hezbollah militants. Lama Fakih, a director of the [Human Rights Watch](46530.md), said that the explosions would constitute an [indiscriminate attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiscriminate_attack) if the IDF had no way of accurately determining the location of the explosive devices since there would be no distinction between civilians and military targets. [UN High Commissioner for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_High_Commissioner_for_Human_Rights) [Volker Türk](71721726.md) said that the attacks violated international human rights since the IDF did not have knowledge regarding the users of the devices or their location and surroundings during the explosions.[^cite_note-631] According to the Syrian Observatory for Human rights, 232 Syrian refugees were killed by the IDF in Lebanon since the start of the Gaza war.[^cite_note-syrianrefugees-84]

#### Targeting of journalists

Main article: [Attacks on journalists during the Israel–Hezbollah conflict (2023–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attacks_on_journalists_during_the_Israel%E2%80%93Hezbollah_conflict_\(2023%E2%80%93present\))

According to the [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe), the intentional targeting of journalists constitutes a war crime.[^cite_note-632] During the conflict, [Reporters Without Borders](526054.md) (RSF) claimed that the Israeli army had deliberately targeted journalists.[^cite_note-RSF-633][^cite_note-634][^cite_note-635] An RSF investigation said that Israel had targeted journalists in two missile strikes on 13 October 2023 that killed Reuters reporter [Issam Abdallah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Issam_Abdallah) and injured four others. These two strikes, 30 seconds apart, hit a group of seven journalists in southern Lebanon who were reporting on the border fighting between Israel and Hezbollah. In a video, the journalists are seen wearing vests and helmets identifying them as "PRESS". The marking was also present on the roof of their car, which exploded after being hit by the second missile.[^cite_note-636] The [Netherlands Organisation for Applied Scientific Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netherlands_Organisation_for_Applied_Scientific_Research), which tests and analyses munitions and weapons, assisted Reuters by examining the material collected at the site of the explosion and found that a piece of metal was the fin of a 120 mm tank round fired 1.34 km away from the border from a [smoothbore tank gun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tank_gun).[^cite_note-reuters20231207-176] A February 2024 report by the [United Nations Interim Force in Lebanon](787766.md) (UNIFIL) concluded that an Israeli tank killed Abdallah when it fired at "clearly identifiable journalists", and that this broke international law. The report "assessed that there was no exchange of fire across the Blue Line at the time of the incident", with no records of any exchange of fire across the border for the 40 minutes before the tank firing.[^cite_note-Bassam-637] The IDF responded to the report by claiming that Hezbollah attacked them, prompting them to retaliate with tank fire.[^cite_note-Bassam-637]

#### IDF's use of white phosphorus

On 31 October 2023, after an investigation, [Amnesty International](18947898.md) stated that an Israeli white phosphorus attack on 16 October was indiscriminate, unlawful, and "must be investigated as a war crime", due to its use on the populated Lebanese town of Dhayra, which injured at least nine civilians.[^cite_note-AmnestyLebanonWP-183][^cite_note-638] On 2 November, Amnesty International stated its investigations into four incidents on 10, 11, 16 and 17 October showed Israel had used white phosphorus munitions.[^cite_note-639] The claim was confirmed by the [Washington Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Post), which identified two white phosphorus shell casings made in the U.S.[^cite_note-640] [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) verified IDF's use of white phosphorus in at least 17 municipalities in Lebanon, including five municipalities where airburst munitions were used over residential areas.[^cite_note-Human-Rights-Watch-2024-641] It also called on the Lebanese government to file a declaration to enable investigations in the [International Criminal Court](14880.md).[^cite_note-Human-Rights-Watch-2024-641]

In southern Lebanon, Israel's white phosphorus bombs have destroyed over 4,500 hectares (45 million m<sup>2</sup>) of forest with economic loses being valued at US\$20 million.[^cite_note-642] The American University of Beirut estimated use of white phosphorus has led to more than 134 forest fires as of June 2024 burning 1,500 hectares (15 million m<sup>2</sup>) of land.[^cite_note-wildfires-in-northern-israel-ignited-by-hezbollah-rockets-364] As of 28 May 2024, the Lebanese Ministry of Public Health said that exposure to white phosphorus had injured at least 173 people.[^cite_note-Human-Rights-Watch-2024-641]

#### Targeting of medical and religious sites

Main article: [Israeli attacks on the Lebanese health sector during the Israel-Hezbollah conflict (2023–present)](78494332.md)

The targeting of hospitals, as well as religious sites, constitutes a war crime.[^cite_note-643] Lebanese health minister [Firass Abiad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firass_Abiad) said that 163 rescuers and health workers were killed and 273 others were injured in Lebanon since the start of Israel–Hezbollah conflict.[^cite_note-644] [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) stated that Israel's "repeated" attacks on medical workers and healthcare facilities were apparent war crimes.[^cite_note-645] The IDF shelled [Meiss Ej Jabal Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meiss_Ej_Jabal_Hospital), injuring a doctor on 10 November 2023. The missiles did not explode but caused damage to the emergency department and several cars. Lebanon's Ministry of Public Health condemned the attack, saying that "Israeli authorities were fully responsible for this unjustifiable act, which would have led to catastrophic results", and called for an investigation.[^cite_note-arabnews20231111-211] Days before, four people were reportedly injured after an Israeli bombing that hit two ambulances. The Lebanese [National News Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_National_News_Agency) said that an Israeli drone strike hit two ambulances belonging to the Risala Scout Association, which is affiliated with the Amal Movement.[^cite_note-macaubusiness20231105-202]

On 26 December 2023, an anti-tank missile shot by [Hezbollah](13919.md) fighters from Lebanon damaged a shed in a church compound in [Iqrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iqrit), but not the church itself, wounding an elderly civilian.[^cite_note-hizb-646] As IDF troops and medical services were working to evacuate him, they were hit by further missiles, which resulted in nine soldiers being wounded, one of them seriously.[^cite_note-647][^cite_note-648] On 11 January 2024, the IDF conducted strikes in the town of [Hanine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanine) and targeted an emergency center affiliated with the Hezbollah-linked [Islamic Health Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Health_Committee?action=edit\&redlink=1). The attack killed two workers from the rescue force and destroyed an ambulance.[^cite_note-649] Other attacks on Islamic Health Authority centers occurred in [Kafr Kila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kafr_Kila,_Lebanon), [Odaisseh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odaisseh) and [Blida](42906975.md) killed seven paramedic and rescue workers and destroyed 17 ambulances.[^cite_note-650]

On 27 March 2024, an Israeli airstrike targeted a paramedic center affiliated with the Islamic Group in [Hebbariye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebbariye), killing seven volunteer paramedics. The airstrike was condemned by the Lebanese Ministry of Health.[^cite_note-Al-Jazeera-20240327-316] Later in the day, Israeli airstrikes in [Tayr Harfa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tayr_Harfa) killed two paramedics from the Islamic Health Society, while strikes in Naqoura killed one from the Amal Movement-affiliated Islamic Risala Scout Association.[^cite_note-AP-News-2024-318] On 7 May 2024, Human Rights Watch declared the 27 March incident as an unlawful attack on civilians and said that they did not find any evidence of military targets at the site that was targeted. Investigations also showed that the IDF used an [MPR 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MPR500) missile to conduct the raid.[^cite_note-651]

On 27 May 2024, an Israeli airstrike near Salah Ghandour Hospital in [Bint Jbeil](6124393.md) killed three civilians. WHO in Lebanon condemned the attack and called for the protection of hospitals and healthcare workers.[^cite_note-652] In October 2024, the head of Lebanon's Civil Defence in the south said Israel was specifically attacking health workers, stating, "We have had 40 ambulances which have been completely destroyed. On top of that 24 rescuing stations have been hit - just in this area."[^cite_note-653] Lebanese health officials stated on 5 October 2024 that fifty health officials had been killed in the prior 72 hours.[^cite_note-654] Lebanon's health minister [Firas Abiad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firas_Abiad) stated the attacks were war crimes and part of Israel's systematic targeting of Lebanon's healthcare system.[^cite_note-655] A November 2024 investigation found Israel had struck in "lethal proximity" to nineteen different hospitals in Lebanon.[^cite_note-656]

#### Use of booby-trapped devices

Experts warned the [2024 Lebanon electronic device attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Lebanon_electronic_device_attacks) potentially violated international humanitarian law.[^cite_note-657] [Josep Borrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josep_Borrell), the European Union's [High Representative for Foreign Affairs and Security Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Representative_for_Foreign_Affairs_and_Security_Policy), questioned the legality of the [pager attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pager_attacks) due to their high collateral damage among civilians, including the deaths of children. [Jeanine Hennis-Plasschaert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeanine_Hennis-Plasschaert), the [United Nations Special Coordinator for Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Special_Coordinator_for_Lebanon), also raised concerns that the attacks were illegal. Belgian deputy prime minister [Petra De Sutter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petra_De_Sutter) went further, calling it a "terror attack".[^cite_note-OliphantConfino-658] [Volker Turk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volker_Turk), the UN human rights chief, stated, "International humanitarian law prohibits the use of booby-trap devices in the form of apparently harmless portable objects".[^cite_note-659]

[Booby traps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Booby_traps) are mostly outlawed under the [Protocol on Mines, Booby-Traps and Other Devices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protocol_on_Mines,_Booby-Traps_and_Other_Devices) ("Amended Protocol II") of the [Convention on Certain Conventional Weapons](3111840.md),[^cite_note-OliphantConfino-658] to which Israel is a party.[^cite_note-Boothby-660] Article 7, paragraph 2 of Amended Protocol II prohibits the use of "booby-traps or other devices in the form of apparently harmless portable objects which are specifically designed and constructed to contain explosive material."[^cite_note-661][^cite_note-OliphantConfino-658][^cite_note-Boothby-660] The [rules of engagement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rules_of_engagement) of some countries, such as the United Kingdom, also ban explosive devices disguised as harmless items.[^cite_note-mod-2004-662] The [United States Department of Defense](7279897.md) Law of War Manual gives watches, cameras, tobacco pipes, and headphones as examples of such items,[^cite_note-Boothby-660] which are prohibited to "prevent the production of large quantities of dangerous objects that can be scattered around and are likely to be attractive to civilians, especially children".[^cite_note-663][^cite_note-OliphantConfino-658] On 22 September 2024, Israeli President [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md) denied any Israeli involvement in the explosions.[^cite_note-independent20240922-472]

### Hezbollah war crimes

#### Killing of Israeli civilians

The [elimination of the State of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calls_for_the_destruction_of_Israel) has been a primary goal for Hezbollah. Hezbollah has been accused of "only \[regarding] the Jews living in Israel as Zionists, who should be killed".[^cite_note-Alagha-664] Throughout the war Hezbollah announced the targeting of civilian homes.[^cite_note-665] On 12 November 2023, Hezbollah fired an anti-tank missile killing an employee of the [Israel Electric Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Electric_Corporation) who was conducting repair work and injured 13 other Israelis, including six other employees of the Israeli Electric Corporation.[^cite_note-666][^cite_note-The-Jerusalem-Post-20231112-214][^cite_note-barrons20231113-215]

The most notable of Hezbollah's attacks on civilians was on 27 July 2024 in the [Majdal Shams attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majdal_Shams_attack), when Hezbollah fired an Iranian-made [Falaq-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falaq-1) rocket at [Majdal Shams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majdal_Shams), a [Druze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druze) town in the Golan Heights. The rocket hit a football pitch [killing 12 Druze children](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majdal_Shams_attack) and injuring at least 42 others.[^cite_note-667][^cite_note-668][^cite_note-Ynetnews-2024-393] Another attack by Hezbollah resulted in civilian casualties on 9 July 2024, when a rocket struck a couple's car just south of their hometown of [Ortal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ortal,_Golan_Heights).[^cite_note-669] Additionally, a couple walking their dog in [Kiryat Shmona](323369.md) was killed by shrapnel from a Hezbollah rocket on 9 October 2024.[^cite_note-670]

On 26 December 2023, a Hezbollah anti-tank missile impacted near a [Channel 13 News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Channel_13_News) team while they were interviewing a farmer at [Dovev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dovev) for an article following a prior Hezbollah assault that killed a 56-year-old employee of the Israel Electric Corporation, and injured five workers who were repairing electric lines.[^cite_note-671] Throughout the conflict, Hezbollah fired thousands of inaccurate munitions at Israel and the Golan Heights, killing and injuring civilians and destroying houses. A December 2024 report by [Amnesty International](18947898.md) found the use of unguided missiles on civilian areas to likely constitute indiscriminate attacks. International humanitarian law states that parties in a conflict must not direct attacks at civilians and must distinguish civilians and civilian infrastructure from military targets.[^cite_note-672]

#### Accusations of Hezbollah using human shields

There have been claims that Hezbollah has intentionally drawn fire to Christian areas in southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-The-Jerusalem-Post-20231022-673] Christians in the town of [Rmaich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rmaich) clashed with Hezbollah due to the organization's attempts to establish military infrastructure in the town. The residents also wrote a complaint letter.[^cite_note-The-Jerusalem-Post-20231022-673] On 26 March 2024, civilians in Rmaich confronted militants and rang their church bells after Hezbollah attempted to place rocket launchers in the town. Hezbollah denied trying to fire rockets from Rmaich.[^cite_note-674] On 30 November 2024, the IDF said that it killed armed Hezbollah operatives near a church in southern Lebanon. A video appearing to be in the town of Khiyam, showed a tunnel shaft used to store weapons at the church.[^cite_note-675]

The Lebanese [Kataeb Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kataeb_Party) criticized Hezbollah for allegedly storing weapons between houses stating that "Hiding weapons between houses does not support Gaza" after an IDF strike on Hezbollah infrastructure in Adloun. Rockets were still exploding about an hour after the strike was reported and the blasts lightly injured three citizens as shrapnel from the explosions flew to surrounding villages. These ammunition depots "open the door to Israeli attacks and turn the Lebanese into human shields", the Kataeb party said.[^cite_note-676]

According to IDF's Arabic spokesman, [Avichay Adraee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avichay_Adraee), Hezbollah-run yellow ambulances have been used to transport fighters and weapons. The Islamic Health Authority's website says it has "provided services for the activity of the Hezbollah fighters against the Zionist occupation."[^cite_note-677] According to UNIFIL, Hezbollah has used the environmental NGO "Green Without Borders" to provide direct cover for Hezbollah's military operations by "unlawful construction of military outposts along the Blue Line."[^cite_note-678] Hezbollah has also launched rockets from UNIFL positions. The IDF reported that 25 rockets were launched from UNIFL positions in October 2024 and said that it discovered hundreds of weapons, including firearms, grenades, and rocket launchers, stored in compounds located near UNIFIL posts in southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-679]

After the [20 September 2024 Beirut attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/20_September_2024_Beirut_attack) which killed major [Radwan Force](69191358.md) commanders in the densely populated Dahieh suburbs, [Daniel Hagari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Hagari) said that the commanders were holding a meeting "gathered underground under a residential building in the heart of the Dahieh neighbourhood, hiding among Lebanese civilians, using them as human shields."[^cite_note-680] On 23 September 2024, the IDF began posting pictures and videos of what it claimed was evidence of Hezbollah placing weapons in people's homes.[^cite_note-681][^cite_note-682] They also released video footage of [secondary explosions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secondary_explosion) after targeting buildings which it says are from Hezbollah munitions stored in residential areas.[^cite_note-683]

## Reactions



Secretary [Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) meets with Lebanese Caretaker Prime Minister Najib Mikati in Amman, Jordan.

### Lebanon

On 6 November 2023, in response to the killing of four civilians in southern Lebanon, Prime Minister [Najib Mikati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Najib_Mikati) announced that his government would submit an urgent complaint to the [UN Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Security_Council) against Israel saying that its "targeting of civilians in its aggression against Lebanon" was a "heinous crime".[^cite_note-684] [Samir Geagea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samir_Geagea), the leader of a [main Christian political party](2113218.md), blamed Hezbollah for attacking Israel and said that it harmed Lebanon while having no impact on Israel's Gaza offensive.[^cite_note-685]

#### Public opinion

| Date | Shi'ite | Sunni | Christian |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2020 | 89% | 8% | 16% |
| 2023 (November) | 93% | 34% | 29% |

As tensions heightened on 8 October 2023 near the Lebanese border, former prime minister [Fouad Siniora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fouad_Siniora) stressed that Lebanon should not be involved "in any hostilities with the Israeli enemy".[^cite_note-MEMRI-20231011-688]

Between 7–9 October 2023, many politicians of long-established Christian parties in Lebanon took a stance against Hezbollah's involvement as well. [Pierre Bou Assi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre_Bou_Assi), a member of the Lebanese Forces's [Strong Republic bloc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strong_Republic_\(Lebanon\)) highlighted the kidnapping of two IDF soldiers which led to the 2006 Lebanon war saying, "Hassan Nasrallah promised, in early July, that the summer would be quiet and thriving—but a few days later, Hezbollah kidnapped two Israeli soldiers and the July war broke out".[^cite_note-MEMRI-20231011-688] The [Kataeb Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kataeb_Party) took a similar stance and condemned the use of Lebanese territory by non-state actors for launching attacks on Israel.[^cite_note-MEMRI-20231011-688]

On 4 December 2023, Hamas announced the organization's official establishment in Lebanon. The Lebanon-based unit was named the "al-Aqsa Flood Vanguards unit".[^cite_note-689][^cite_note-MEMRI-20231212-690] The establishment of the Vanguards during the [2023 Hamas-led attack on Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Hamas-led_attack_on_Israel) faced severe criticism from various political figures and factions within Lebanon. Many argued that such an organization, with potential military implications, could infringe upon Lebanon's sovereignty and violate international resolutions, particularly the [Taif Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taif_Agreement). Critics, including members of the Christian [Lebanese Forces](2113218.md) Party and other political figures, voiced concerns about a potential recurrence of historical issues related to armed Palestinian factions operating from Lebanese territory.[^cite_note-MEMRI-20231212-690][^cite_note-Al-Aqsa-28]

Screens at [Beirut–Rafic Hariri International Airport](12869180.md) were hacked allegedly by the Christian group [Jnud al-Rab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jnud_al-Rab) to display an anti-Hezbollah message. Jnud al-Rab denied any involvement.[^cite_note-691]

On 28 January 2024, [Bechara Boutros al-Rahi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bechara_Boutros_al-Rahi), [Maronite Patriarch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maronite_Patriarch) and head of the [Maronite Church](66391078.md), stated that residents of southern Lebanon refuse to be used as "sacrificial lambs" in what he described as a "culture of death", implicitly referencing Hezbollah's border activities. He described the hardships faced by the people, including psychological strain and disrupted education for children, and said they refuse to be hostages or human shields.[^cite_note-692] On 24 March 2024, a video showed a nun at a Lebanese Christian school asking students to pray for Hezbollah men defending Lebanon. The video went viral, with some praising the video and others criticizing it.[^cite_note-693] [Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinians_in_Lebanon) in [Shatila refugee camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shatila_refugee_camp) told [Al Jazeera](30204052.md) that they would fight on the side of Hezbollah and the [Axis of Resistance](36658251.md) in a war against Israel, but worried for their families and civilians as they feared that Israel would deliberately target densely populated civilian areas.[^cite_note-694]

### Iraq

Main article: [Gaza war § Iraq](74998519.md#Iraq)

On 9 January 2024, the [Kata'ib Hezbollah](23626646.md) spokesperson Jafar al-Husseini warned that the [Islamic Resistance in Iraq](75274618.md) would help Hezbollah fight Israel if war erupted between the two sides.[^cite_note-695] This statement was a few weeks after the Islamic Resistance in Iraq claimed responsibility for a drone attack on a [Karish rig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karish_gas_field) which Lebanon [claims to hold sovereignty to](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli%E2%80%93Lebanese_maritime_border_dispute).[^cite_note-696] On 23 June 2024, the spokesperson to [Kata'ib Sayyid al-Shuhada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kata'ib_Sayyid_al-Shuhada) Kadhem al-Fartousi said the brigade will join Hezbollah's side if Israel decides to launch a full-blown war in Lebanon.[^cite_note-697][^cite_note-698] On 24 June 2024, [Qais al-Khazali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qais_al-Khazali), the leader of [Asa'ib Ahl al-Haq](26106154.md), stated in a televised speech that if the U.S. continued to support Israel in expanding the war to Lebanon and Hezbollah, the group would begin to attack US interests in Iraq and the Middle East.[^cite_note-699]

### United States



U.S. secretary of defense [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin) and Israeli defense minister [Yoav Gallant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Gallant) in Tel Aviv, Israel, 18 December 2023

On 4 November 2023, [United States Secretary of State](32293.md) [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) thanked prime minister [Najib Mikati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Najib_Mikati) during a meeting in [Amman](45007.md) "in preventing Lebanon from being pulled into a war that the Lebanese people do not want".[^cite_note-700] [U.S. Secretary of Defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Secretary_of_Defense) [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin) expressed concern about Israel's role in ongoing tensions with Lebanon.[^cite_note-701] On 30 September 2024, [the Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon) said it would be deploying a "few thousand" more troops to the Middle East.[^cite_note-702] On 1 October 2024, Defense Secretary Austin told Israeli defense minister [Yoav Gallant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Gallant) that the United States supports Israel's [ground offensive against Hezbollah](78006796.md) in southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-703]

The U.S. Embassy in Beirut announced on 27 September 2024 that it was "not evacuating U.S. citizens at this time."[^cite_note-704] In response, U.S. Representative [Rashida Tlaib](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashida_Tlaib) stated, the State Department was "leaving Americans behind and failing to protect their own citizens".[^cite_note-705] American citizens in Lebanon stated they were being treated like "lesser US citizens".[^cite_note-706] Lebanese Americans filed a class-action lawsuit against the State Department in the hopes of expediting an evacuation.[^cite_note-707]

### Other states



Demonstration against the bombing of Lebanon and Gaza in Copenhagen, Denmark, 24 September 2024

[French Armed Forces Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_the_Armed_Forces_\(France\)) [Sébastien Lecornu](54364868.md) announced that [France](5843419.md) would donate dozens of [armoured vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armored_car_\(military\)) to the [Lebanese military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_military) to help it carry out patrol missions.[^cite_note-708] Since June 2024, the [foreign ministries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_ministries) of [Canada](5042916.md),[^cite_note-709] [Kuwait](7515890.md),[^cite_note-710] [Germany](11867.md), the [Netherlands](21148.md)[^cite_note-711] and [North Macedonia](23564616.md)[^cite_note-712] issued statements urging their citizens to leave Lebanon due to fears of an escalation of the conflict. The [Canadian Armed Forces](182792.md) said that it was planning to evacuate its 20,000 citizens from Lebanon in the event of an escalation, although it would require support from its allies.[^cite_note-713] On 8 November 2023, two men were arrested by Brazilian police in [São Paulo](390875.md) following a warning from [Mossad](27619007.md) that Hezbollah was planning an attack against the country's [Jewish community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jews_in_Brazil). Searches were also conducted in [Brasília](4752.md) and [Minas Gerais](222651.md) in connection with the alleged plot.[^cite_note-714]

### Economic

As a result of the fighting, [Swiss International Air Lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swiss_International_Air_Lines) and [Lufthansa](97733.md) suspended flights to [Beirut](37428.md), while Lebanon's flag carrier [Middle East Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_East_Airlines) relocated five of its 24 aircraft to [Istanbul](3391396.md) as a precaution. Australia, Canada, France, Germany, Spain and the United Kingdom advised against travelling to Lebanon, whilst the British embassy withdrew family members of its staff.[^cite_note-715]

### Humanitarian response

The Lebanon Humanitarian Fund launched its reserve allocation that includes up to US\$4 million to support its partners to help aid those who have been displaced or still in conflict zones.[^cite_note-ReliefWeb-2023-716]

## Rearmament of Hezbollah

Event though the ceasefire agreement was signed by both parties, immediately after Hezbollah began its rearmament. According to Israeli and Arab intelligence sources, the organization is using the Beirut seaport and its old smuggling land routes from Syria, for bringing in long range missiles, antitank missiles, rockets, and artillery shells. It is also reported that, Hezbollah is manufacturing weapons. The efforts take place mainly in the suburbs of Beirut and the Bekaa Valley.[^cite_note-717][^cite_note-718]
